# Roadmap: tautological rings and motivic structures in the cohomology of moduli of stable curves

**Summary.** This roadmap develops the rational cohomology of the moduli stacks `M̄_{g,n}` of stable pointed curves through the operations that build it from smaller moduli spaces. From the stacks, their boundary strata, clutching and forgetful morphisms, cotangent lines and Hodge bundle, it builds the tautological rings, the semi-tautological extensions (STEs) of Canning–Larson–Payne, the boundary-induction machinery of Arbarello–Cornalba, the Chow–Künneth generation property, the genus-four point-count sieve of Bergström–Faber–Payne and the stable-graph assembly of Getzler–Kapranov. Its end theorems are: odd cohomology of every `M̄_{g,n}` vanishes in degrees at most nine (BFP, Theorem 1.1); `M̄_{4,n}` has polynomial point count with pure Tate cohomology for `n ≤ 3` (BFP, Theorem 1.4); every degree-`k` cohomology group is contained in an STE generated by finitely many explicit groups, so only finitely many simple Hodge structures or Galois representations occur in each degree (CLP, Theorem 1.3 and Corollary 1.4); `H⁴(M̄_{g,n})` is tautological for all `g, n`, `H⁶` for `g ≥ 10`, and even homology in degrees at most fourteen is tautological (CLP, Theorem 1.5); homology in degrees thirteen and fifteen is generated from the genus-one cusp classes (CLP, Theorems 1.6–1.7); `M_{7,n}` has the Chow–Künneth generation property and tautological Chow ring for `n ≤ 3` (CLP, Theorem 1.10); and the classification `H¹³(M̄_{g,n})^ss ≅ ⊕ L S₁₂`, `H¹⁴(M̄_{g,n})^ss ≅ ⊕ L⁷`, and for `g ≥ 2` `H¹⁵(M̄_{g,n})^ss ≅ ⊕ L² S₁₂`, as Hodge structures and as `ℓ`-adic Galois representations (CLP, Theorem 1.1); and for `n ≤ 11` every `H^k(M̄_{3,n})^ss` is a polynomial in `L` and `S₁₂` (CLP, Theorem 1.9).

## Scope and ownership

This roadmap owns the cohomology theory of the curve moduli stacks built from their tautological operations: tautological rings and kappa classes, the stable-pair order, the boundary exact sequences and the Arbarello–Cornalba induction, the marking filtration and local-system quotient, semi-tautological extensions and their finite generation, the Chow–Künneth generation property and its permanence, the genus-seven gonality stratification with its tetragonal and Mukai models, the hyperelliptic and genus-four quadric point counts, marking twists, the weight-graded equivariant Euler characteristic, the stable-graph transform and the Getzler–Kapranov formula applied to curve moduli, the cusp-decorated graph calculations, and every end theorem listed in the summary. StableReductionPartII §7.11 supplies the domain stacks and algebraic maps only and assigns tautological rings, motivic and cohomological structures, cycle maps, graph complexes and push–pull theorems on curve moduli to this roadmap.

Five families of inputs that no roadmap owns are owned here because their only consumer is this cohomology theory: the mapping-class-group inputs (the identification of the rational cohomology of the moduli stack with that of the pure mapping class group, Harer's virtual cohomological dimension, its top-degree vanishing, homological stability and the stable cohomology of Madsen–Weiss and Looijenga, §§2.1–2.2 and 3.4); the classification of quadric surfaces over finite fields with their stabilizer orders (§8.4); the character-sum evaluations over binary forms that give the hyperelliptic moments (§8.2); the genus-two inputs, namely the Torelli identification of the compact-type moduli of genus two with the moduli stack `A₂` of principally polarized abelian surfaces (§3.6), Petersen's computation of the semisimplified compactly supported cohomology of the symplectic local systems `V_{a,b}` on `A₂` (Petersen, abelian surfaces, Theorem 2.1), used for their Euler characteristics with `a + b ≤ 12` in §3.6 and for the pure cohomology of weights thirteen and fifteen of `M_{2,n}` in §11.1, where local systems with `a + b ≤ 14` enter, and Petersen's decomposition of the cohomology of the pointed compact-type moduli `M^{ct}_{2,n}` into cohomology of local systems on `M^{ct}_2` and on `Sym² M_{1,1}`, with his theorem that the even cohomology of `M̄_{2,n}` is tautological for `n < 20` (Petersen, genus two, Theorems 2.1 and 3.8), used in §§4.3 and 11.1; and the tautological classes of special loci in the Chow–Künneth ranges of §§5.4 and 6.9, namely the loci of pointed curves with a cover of `ℙ¹` with marked ramification, among them the hyperelliptic loci (Faber–Pandharipande, recalled in Canning–Larson, Lemma 8.1, p. 26, and proof of Corollary 7.5, p. 22), and the plane-quintic locus in genus six (Faber, recalled in Canning–Larson, proof of Lemma 10.14, p. 39).

The following are imported, not planned here.

- The stacks `M̄_{g,n}` and `M_{g,n}`, their forgetful morphism and normal-crossings boundary are SchemeAndStackFoundations §4.15, on the stack foundations of §§1.18–1.23. Their realization as pointed moduli groupoids, the universal curve, clutching morphisms, relabelling, the normalized boundary strata with graph automorphisms, the marking cotangent lines with their forgetful correction, and the Hodge bundle are StableReductionPartII §0.4, §2.248, §§3.1–3.3, 3.7, 3.10–3.11, §§5.1, 5.10–5.11 and the export §7.11. Dual graphs of nodal curves and their genus formula are StableReduction, Layer 1; stability of pointed prestable curves, and the absence of infinitesimal automorphisms on a stable geometric fibre, are its Layer 3.
- Vistoli's rational Chow groups of separated Deligne–Mumford stacks, with proper pushforward, flat pullback, exterior products, Chern classes and refined Gysin maps, are VirtualFundamentalClass §§2.1–2.4; the tautological rings of §§0.1–0.2 are formed in them, and `ψᵢ = c₁(Lᵢ)` for the cotangent lines of StableReductionPartII §3.3 is the first Chern class of VirtualFundamentalClass §2.3, the Chow theory StableReductionPartII itself consumes. The Edidin–Graham equivariant Chow groups of quotient stacks `[X/G]`, with their operations and `A^*_{GL_n} = ℤ[c₁, …, c_n]`, are EquivariantIntersectionTheory §§2.1–2.3, and their rational comparison with Vistoli's groups on Deligne–Mumford quotient stacks is StableReductionPartII §5.15. Rational Chow groups of finite-type algebraic stacks stratified by quotient stacks, on which Layer 5 defines the Chow–Künneth generation property, are requested from SchemeAndStackFoundations (gap:stack-rational-chow); §0.6 identifies them with the two theories above where these apply.
- The quotient presentation of the stack of smooth hyperelliptic curves of genus `g ≥ 2` over a field of characteristic not dividing `4(g + 1)` is EquivariantIntersectionTheory §3.3; §8.1 owns only its extension over `ℤ[1/2]`, which covers the odd characteristics dividing `g + 1`, and the point count.
- Finite groupoid mass, stack point counts and traces, twisted counts, signed Frobenius configurations, inverse zeta functions, termination for proper even cohomology, the Hasse–Weil sieve, exact and approximate polynomial counts, the local cutoff, and smooth proper DM purity are WeilConjectures, WC.1, WC.5 and WC.6. This roadmap applies them to the curve families and moduli stacks; it does not restate them.
- The ℓ-adic DM-stack cohomology over finite fields, the rational coarse comparison, smooth proper DM duality and purity, and the weight spectral sequence of a normal crossings compactification, including its specialization to the boundary of `M̄_{g,n}` with orientation characters, are DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24, stated over finite fields; the stack Artin comparison and smooth proper transport that carry them to the `G_ℚ`-realization over `ℚ̄` are WeilConjectures, WC.1 (Conventions, **Cohomology**). Hard Lefschetz is DeligneWeightsAndPurity §9.6.
- Parabolic realizations of cusp forms, with their Hodge filtration and λ-adic realizations, are AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations §1.2; level-one cusp-form dimensions are Mathlib's; level-one period polynomials are Tau Ceti's.
- Abstract pure and mixed Hodge structures and Tate twists are Tau Ceti's `TauCeti.Hodge` library; the representation ring and characters of a finite group are Tau Ceti's `TauCeti.repRing` and `TauCeti.repRingCharacter`; half-spin representations are Tau Ceti's `TauCeti.spinPlus` and its companions.
- Partitions, tabloids and permutation modules, Young symmetrizers and Specht modules are SchurWeyl, Layers 0–3, which consume them from Tau Ceti, and the hook-length dimensions are its Layer 5; Schur polynomials, the fixed-degree Frobenius characteristic and Pieri rules are SchurWeyl, Layer 7; symplectic Brauer contractions and harmonic tensors are SchurWeyl, Layer 9.
- Special λ-rings and Adams operations are SchemeKTheoryOperations §§6.2.1–6.2.2; augmentation-complete logarithms and exponentials are HabiroNahmSeries §5.1.
- Coherent Grassmannians and their smoothness are AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry §1.3; rigidification by a normal inertia subgroup is AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry §5.1.
- Recognition of semisimple Galois representations by Frobenius traces on a density-one set of primes is ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations §§5.1 and 5.3.
- The Teichmüller uniformization of the pointed moduli stack is MappingClassGroupsAndCanonicalRepresentations §2.3. The comparison of the constant rational cohomology of an orbifold with that of its classifying space, the realization of the nerve of a proper étale presentation, and with that of its coarse space is Orbifolds O4. The cohomology of the base of a covering by a free action with contractible total space is group cohomology by Tau Ceti's `IsQuotientCoveringMap.singularCohomologyIsoGroupCohomology`. §2.1 applies both to the action groupoid of the pure mapping class group on Teichmüller space.
- Torelli morphisms at full level `N ≥ 3` and the compactified Torelli map on coarse spaces are StableReductionPartII §§6.3, 6.12 and 6.17; §3.6 owns only the genus-two identification of the level-one compact-type stack `M₂^{ct}` with `A₂`, which those targets do not state.
- The moduli stack `A₂` of principally polarized abelian surfaces, with its universal family, is PELModuli §M5.2 at genus two, principal type and level one.
- The Brill–Noether existence theorem and its consequence `gon ≤ ⌊(g + 3)/2⌋` for function fields over algebraically closed fields are CanonicalModelsAndGonality §5.4; the dictionary between function fields and smooth proper curves that carries the bound to curves is AlgebraicCurves Layer 12. §6.1 applies them at `g = 7` and owns only the stratification of `M_{7,n}`. Noether's theorem, the scheme form of the canonical embedding and the genus-four canonical ideal are CanonicalModelsAndGonality §§3.4, 9.1 and 3.9(b); §8.5 owns only the uniqueness and rationality of the quadric over the base field and the converse.

Two statements of CLP are conjectures and are not targets: every even `H^k(M̄_{g,n})` with `k ≤ 20` is tautological (CLP, Conjecture 1.8, p. 3), and for `g ≥ 2` the group `H¹⁵(M̄_{g,n})` lies in the STE generated by `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` (CLP, Conjecture 7.12, p. 28). CLP, Lemma 3.2, p. 10, is printed for every degree `k`; for even `k ≥ 24` its proof leaves unproved that the two-cusp space `T^k_n` of §4.6 lies in the STE generated by the three printed families, and no source proves it. That containment is not a target: §4.6 proves the printed lemma for odd `k` and for `k ≤ 23`, where `T^k_n = 0`, and proves it for every `k` with `T^k_n` adjoined to the generators. The degree-six criterion of §10.6 is a proved conditional implication; its finite perfectness hypothesis is not asserted. CLP Remark 7.8 announces a complete presentation of the cusp-decorated graph relations; no target here depends on it.

## Conventions

- **Stable pairs.** A stable pair is `(g, n) ∈ ℕ²` with `2g − 2 + n > 0`; `d = d_{g,n} = 3g − 3 + n` is the dimension of `M̄_{g,n}` and of `M_{g,n}`. Statements over all `g, n` range over stable pairs only. There is no forgetful morphism to an unstable target; in particular there is none from `M_{1,1}` to `M_{1,0}`.
- **Stacks.** `M̄_{g,n}` is the smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stack over `ℤ` of stable `n`-pointed curves of genus `g`, with ordered distinct markings; `M_{g,n}` is the open substack of smooth curves, generally not proper. Normalized boundary strata have compact factors `M̄_{g_v,n_v}`; strata of the open stratification have factors `M_{g_v,n_v}`. `M_{0,4}` is `ℙ¹` minus three points and `M̄_{0,4} ≅ ℙ¹`.
- **Cohomology.** `H^*(X)` is rational Betti cohomology of the associated complex analytic stack with its mixed Hodge structure, or `ℚ_ℓ`-étale cohomology of `X_ℚ̄` with its continuous `G_ℚ`-action; a statement made "in both realizations" holds for each, compatibly with the Artin comparison. `H_c^*` is compactly supported cohomology. For smooth `U`, `W_k H^k(U)` is the lowest-weight part of `H^k(U)` and `Gr^W_k H_c^k(U)` the top-weight part of `H_c^k(U)`; for `U` of pure dimension `d`, Poincaré duality pairs complementary degrees, `Gr^W_k H_c^k(U) ≅ (W_{2d−k} H^{2d−k}(U))^∨(−d)` and `W_k H^k(U) ≅ (Gr^W_{2d−k} H_c^{2d−k}(U))^∨(−d)`. Witness: for `U = 𝔸¹` (`d = 1`), `W₀H⁰ = ℚ` while `Gr^W_0 H_c^0 = 0`, so the two lowest and top pieces of the same degree are not dual; the complementary pairs are `Gr^W_0 H_c^0 = 0 = W₂H²` and `Gr^W_2 H_c^2 = ℚ(−1) = (W₀H⁰)^∨(−1)`. Superscript `ss` is semisimplification; Hodge structures of smooth proper DM stacks are semisimple, so `ss` matters only for Galois representations. No statement about a semisimplification splits a Galois extension. DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 is stated over finite fields; in the `ℓ`-adic realization over `ℚ̄` the weight filtration of `H_c^*(U)`, for `U` smooth with a normal crossings compactification `X̄` (such as `M_{g,n} ⊂ M̄_{g,n}`), is the filtration defined by the resolution of `j_!ℚ_ℓ` by the normalized boundary strata with their orientation characters (§10.7), and that of `H^*(U)` is its dual under Poincaré duality, so that `W_kH^k(U)` is the image of `H^k(X̄)`; both are defined over the field of definition and are `G_ℚ`-stable for `M_{g,n}`. The `E₁` terms of the spectral sequence of this resolution are cohomology groups of smooth proper stacks and its `d₁` is the alternating sum of restriction maps, so the stack Artin comparison of WeilConjectures, WC.1, identifies it with the Hodge weight spectral sequence of gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge, which degenerates at `E₂`, and the smooth proper transport of WC.1 identifies its `E₁` terms with those of DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 for the reduction modulo `p ≠ ℓ`, where they are pure. The statements of DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 on duality, Künneth, purity and the weight spectral sequence are used in the `G_ℚ`-realization only through these two comparisons. Witness: `W₁₁H¹¹(M_{1,11})` is the image of `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11}) = S₁₂`, a `G_ℚ`-stable subspace of rank two; for `M_{0,4}` the row `C^•_0(0, 4)` of §10.7 gives `Gr^W_0 H_c¹(M_{0,4}) = ℚ_ℓ³/ℚ_ℓ ≅ ℚ_ℓ²` with trivial `G_ℚ`-action, and the Hodge realization gives `ℚ²` of type `(0, 0)`.
- **Tate twist and Frobenius.** `L = ℚ(−1) = H²(ℙ¹)`; geometric Frobenius acts on `L` by `q`. A codimension-`c` cycle class lies in `H^{2c}` with Hodge type `(c, c)`. Witness: on `ℙ¹` over `𝔽_q`, `H²` has geometric Frobenius eigenvalue `q` and the cycle class of a point spans it; replacing `L` by `ℚ(1)` would give eigenvalue `q⁻¹`.
- **Homology.** For a smooth proper `X` of pure dimension `d`, `H_k(X) = H^{2d−k}(X)(d)`. For smooth `U`, Borel–Moore homology is `H_k^{BM}(U) = H^{2d−k}(U)(d)`, dual to `H_c^k(U)`. Following CLP, for an STE `S` the space `S_k(M̄_{g,n})` means the cohomological subspace `S^{2d−k}(M̄_{g,n})`; an STE is never redefined as a homology ring. Witness: for `M̄_{0,4} = ℙ¹`, `H₀ = H²(1)` is one-dimensional of weight zero.
- **Cusp realizations.** `S₁₂ = H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` and `S₁₆ = W₁₅H¹⁵(M_{1,15})`, each of rank two, of Hodge types `(11,0),(0,11)` and `(15,0),(0,15)`. More generally `S_{k+1}` denotes the parabolic realization of level-one cusp forms of weight `k + 1`, of rank `2 dim S_{k+1}(SL₂(ℤ))`.
- **Point counts.** A finite-field count of a stack is groupoid mass, the sum of `1/#Aut` over isomorphism classes of rational points (WeilConjectures, WC.1, finite groupoid mass). Polynomial point count means the polynomial-count condition of WeilConjectures, WC.5 (exact polynomial point count): a polynomial `P ∈ ℚ[T]` with `N(q) = P(q)` at every prime power `q`, and no condition at other arguments. An `S_n`-equivariant polynomial count of a stack `X` with an action of `S_n`, such as `M̄_{g,n}`, `M_{g,n}` or the hyperelliptic `H_{g,n}` with the relabelling of the markings, is a family of polynomials `P_σ ∈ ℚ[T]`, `σ ∈ S_n`, such that the mass of the twist `X^σ(𝔽_q)` of §1.1 is `P_σ(q)` at every prime power `q`. Conjugate permutations give isomorphic twists (§1.1), so the coefficient of `T^j` is a rational class function `a_j` on `S_n`, a rational combination of irreducible characters, and the count is written `Σ_j ch(a_j) T^j` with the Frobenius characteristic in Schur functions; that each `a_j` is a virtual character is not part of the definition (§1.5, second Check). Witness: for `M̄_{0,4}` every twist is a form of `ℙ¹` over `𝔽_q`, which is `ℙ¹`, so `P_σ = T + 1` for all `σ ∈ S₄`, and the count is `s_{(4)} T + s_{(4)}`. For `M_{0,4}` the rational points of the twisted boundary are the boundary points fixed by the image of `σ` in `S₃`, so `P_σ = T − 2` for `σ` in the Klein four-group, `T` for a transposition or a four-cycle and `T + 1` for a three-cycle; the count is `s_{(4)} T − s_{(2,2)}`, and a definition ignoring the twist would give `T − 2` for every `σ`.
- **Symmetric groups.** `T_λ` is the irreducible rational representation (Specht module) of `S_n` attached to a partition `λ ⊢ n`; `s_λ` its Schur function; `T_{(n)}` is trivial. `V_λ` denotes a symplectic local system (§3.1), a different object.
- **Coefficients and characteristic.** Chow groups have rational coefficients, `A^*(X) = CH^*(X) ⊗ ℚ`: on schemes those of SchemeAndStackFoundations Layer 5, on separated Deligne–Mumford stacks Vistoli's groups (VirtualFundamentalClass §2.1), in which the tautological rings live, and on stacks stratified by quotient stacks those of gap:stack-rational-chow, identified with the former and with `A^G_*(X) ⊗ ℚ` by §0.6. On a quotient stack `[X/G]` the equivariant group `A^G_i(X)` sits in dimension `i − dim G`. The algebraic-cycle constructions of Layers 5–7 are over fields of characteristic zero, except the definitions and permanence properties of §§5.1–5.2, which, like §0.6, hold over an arbitrary field. The point counts of Layer 1 (§§1.1, 1.5 and 1.6) and of Layers 8–9 range over all finite fields, including characteristic two.

## Exact supplier contracts

Each contract states the output consumed and what is assumed of it. Where a supplier does not state the needed output, the requirement is stated as `What <Supplier> must provide (gap:<slug>)`.

### From Mathlib

- `CuspForm`, `ModularForm`, `CuspForm.rank_eq_one_of_weight_eq_twelve`, `CuspForm.rank_eq_zero_of_weight_lt_twelve`, `CuspForm.discriminantEquiv` (`CuspForm 𝒮ℒ k ≃ₗ[ℂ] ModularForm 𝒮ℒ (k − 12)`) and `ModularForm.dimension_level_one` (`Mathlib/NumberTheory/ModularForms/LevelOne/DimensionFormula.lean`) give, at level one, `dim S₁₂ = 1` directly and `dim S₁₄ = dim M₂ = 0`, `dim S₁₆ = dim M₄ = 1` through the discriminant isomorphism and the dimension formula. These are complex-analytic carriers; the moduli realization is §0.5.
- `IsPrimePow` (`Mathlib/Algebra/IsPrimePow.lean`) fixes the quantifier of every polynomial-count statement through WeilConjectures, WC.5.
- `quadraticChar` and `quadraticChar_sum_zero` (`Mathlib/NumberTheory/LegendreSymbol/QuadraticChar/Basic.lean`) supply the quadratic character of a finite field of odd characteristic for §8.2.
- `Polynomial`, `Equiv.Perm.cycleType`, `Nat.Partition`, `Representation` and `FDRep` are used with their library meanings.
- `CategoryTheory.Skeleton` (`Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Skeletal.lean`), the isomorphism classes of a category with the automorphism groups of chosen representatives, and `CategoryTheory.ObjectProperty.FullSubcategory` (`Mathlib/CategoryTheory/ObjectProperty/FullSubcategory.lean`) carry the groupoid mass of §9.5, a `finsum` over that skeleton.

### From Tau Ceti

- `TauCeti.spinPlus`, `TauCeti.spinPlusAction`, `TauCeti.spinPlusSubrep` (`TauCeti/RepresentationTheory/Spin/HalfSpin/Basic.lean`) and `TauCeti.finrank_spinPlus` (`TauCeti/RepresentationTheory/Spin/Dimension.lean`): the even half-spin module of a split quadratic space, its Clifford action and invariant subrepresentation, and its rank `2^{r−1}` for a polarization of rank `r`, which is `16` for the ten-dimensional split space. Nothing here asserts descent through a central quotient.
- `TauCeti.repRing` and `TauCeti.repRingCharacter` (`TauCeti/RepresentationTheory/RepresentationRing/Basic.lean`): the split Grothendieck ring of finite-dimensional representations of a finite group over a field and its character map to class functions. For finite groups in characteristic zero split and exact relations agree.
- `TauCeti.finrank_periodPolynomials_eq_finrank_modularForm_add_finrank_cuspForm` (`TauCeti/NumberTheory/ModularForms/LevelOne/PeriodPolynomial/Finrank.lean`): the level-one period-polynomial dimension count used in the rational Eichler–Shimura comparison of §0.5.
- The `TauCeti.Hodge` library (`TauCeti/Geometry/Hodge/`, including `Mixed/` and `Tate/`): pure, mixed and polarizable Hodge structures, morphisms, strictness and Tate twists, as the carriers of Hodge realizations.
- `TauCeti.DualGraph`, `TauCeti.DualGraph.arithmeticGenus`, `TauCeti.DualGraph.IsStable`, `TauCeti.DualGraph.clutch`, `TauCeti.DualGraph.selfClutch`, `TauCeti.DualGraph.smoothing` and `TauCeti.DualGraph.Iso` (`TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/Curves/DualGraph/{Basic,Clutching,Smoothing,Iso}.lean`): the dual graph of a pointed nodal curve with vertex genera and the components carrying the markings, its arithmetic genus `Σ_v g_v + b₁`, stability as positivity of every log-canonical degree, external and self clutching, the smoothing of a set of nodes, which for one edge `e` is the contraction `Γ′ → Γ′/e` of §10.7, and isomorphisms of dual graphs; these implement the dual graphs of StableReduction, Layer 1. Assumed: an isomorphism in `TauCeti.DualGraph.Iso` carries the unordered pair of endpoints of each edge and so does not record the exchange of the two branches of a self-node; the graph automorphism groups of §§1.3, 10.7 and 12.6 act on flags, as in the boundary normalization of StableReductionPartII §3.10, where the one-loop graph of type `(1, 1)` has the branch exchange as an automorphism of order two.
- `TauCeti.AlgebraicGeometry.PointedPrestableFamily` (`TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/Curves/PointedPrestable/Basic.lean`): prestable families of curves over a scheme with ordered disjoint markings in the smooth locus, with pullback and reindexing; no stability condition is part of it, and the moduli stacks are those of SchemeAndStackFoundations §4.15. Its morphisms (`TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/Curves/PointedPrestable/Category.lean`) are morphisms of total spaces over the base that carry each marking to the marking of the same index, so over `Spec k` its isomorphisms and automorphism groups are those of the groupoid `M̄_{g,n}(k)` once stability is imposed (§9.5).
- `TauCeti.AlgebraicGeometry.PrestableMap.const` and `TauCeti.AlgebraicGeometry.PrestableMap.infinitesimalAut` (`TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/Curves/StableMaps/{Basic,Automorphism}.lean`): the constant prestable map of a pointed prestable family through a section of the target, and, over `Spec R`, the automorphisms of the base change of a prestable map to the dual numbers `R[ε]` that restrict to the identity modulo `ε`, which fix every marking. `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.genus` (`TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/Cohomology/Genus.lean`): `dim_k H¹(X, 𝒪_X)` for a scheme over a field `k` with finite-dimensional `H¹`. They state stability and genus over a field in §9.5.
- `IsQuotientCoveringMap.singularCohomologyIsoGroupCohomology` (`TauCeti/AlgebraicTopology/Cohomology/GroupCohomology.lean`): for the quotient covering map `p : E → B` of an action of a group `G` with contractible total space `E`, the singular cohomology `Hⁿ(B; M)` with coefficients in a module `M` over a commutative ring is Mathlib's `groupCohomology` of the trivial representation `M`, in every degree. Used with `M = ℚ` in §2.1; it is a statement about topological spaces, and the identification of the cohomology of the analytic stack with that of its Borel construction is Orbifolds O4, applied in §2.1.

### From TauCetiRoadmap.SchemeAndStackFoundations

Consumed: §§1.18–1.23 (stacks in groupoids, algebraic and Deligne–Mumford stacks, inertia, quotient stacks, quasi-coherent modules, coarse spaces and Keel–Mori); §4.15 (the moduli stack of stable pointed curves, its forgetful morphism, its properness, and its smoothness over `ℤ` of relative dimension `3g − 3 + n` with normal-crossings boundary); §4.17 (strict normal crossings divisors); Layer 5, §§5.1–5.8 (Chow groups of schemes, proper pushforward, flat pullback, localization, Chern classes, projective bundles, refined Gysin maps, intersection products and Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch for schemes). Assumed: rational coefficients are the separate carrier `CH_k(X)_ℚ`, and projective bundles follow the line convention of §5.

What SchemeAndStackFoundations must provide (gap:stack-rational-chow): for finite-type algebraic stacks over a field that are stratified by quotient stacks, a class closed under products that contains stacks which are neither Deligne–Mumford nor global quotients (such as the products `X × Y` with an arbitrary test stack `Y` in §5.1), rational Chow groups extending those of Layer 5 on schemes, with flat and lci pullback, pushforward along proper morphisms of Deligne–Mumford type that need not be representable, exterior products natural under product with every such stack, the localization sequence, homotopy invariance for affine bundles, the projective- and Grassmann-bundle formulas, Chern classes of vector bundles, the intersection product on smooth such stacks with the projection formula, and Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch for the universal curve over `M̄_{g,n}`. Vistoli's groups and the equivariant groups are not asked of this supplier: they are VirtualFundamentalClass §2.1 and EquivariantIntersectionTheory §2.1, and their agreement with these groups is §0.6.

### From TauCetiRoadmap.EquivariantIntersectionTheory

Consumed: Layer 2, §§2.1–2.3 (for `G` smooth affine over a field and `X` quasi-projective with a `G`-linearised ample invertible sheaf, its Conventions 1 and 5: the equivariant Chow groups `A^G_*(X)` of Totaro's approximation, independent of the approximation; proper pushforward, flat and lci pullback, the localisation sequence, equivariant Chern classes, the intersection product for smooth `X`, homotopy invariance for equivariant vector and affine bundles, and invariance under change of presentation; `A^*_{GL_n} = ℤ[c₁, …, c_n]`, `A^*_{𝔾_m^r} = ℤ[t₁, …, t_r]` and, for `n` invertible, `A^*_{μ_n} = ℤ[t]/(n t)`); §3.3 (the presentation of the stack of smooth hyperelliptic curves of genus `g ≥ 2` as `[A_sm(1, 2g + 2)/(GL₂/μ_{g+1})]` over a field of characteristic not dividing `4(g + 1)`). Assumed: these groups are integral; this roadmap uses them after `⊗ ℚ`, through §0.6.

What EquivariantIntersectionTheory must provide (gap:equivariant-grr): for `G` smooth affine over a field, a `G`-equivariant projective morphism `f : X → Y` of smooth quasi-projective schemes with `G`-linearised ample invertible sheaves (Convention 5), and a `G`-equivariant vector bundle `E` on `X` whose higher direct images `R^i f_* E` are vector bundles, the identity `ch(Σ_i (−1)^i [R^i f_* E]) · td(T_Y) = f_*(ch(E) · td(T_X))` in the completed rational equivariant Chow ring `∏_c A^c_G(Y) ⊗ ℚ`, with equivariant Chern characters and Todd classes, obtained by applying SchemeAndStackFoundations §5.8 on the mixed spaces of §2.1, on which `f` induces projective morphisms of smooth quasi-projective varieties, in the manner of §2.2. It is applied in §6.5, through §0.6, to the finite flat degree-four cover `α : C → ℙ` of the universal curve over the tetragonal Hurwitz stack onto its `ℙ¹`-fibration and to the fibration `ℙ → H_{4,g,n}` (§6.3), on the framed presentation of §6.3, where the `SL₂`-lift of the Hurwitz stack, its fibration and its universal curve are the quotients by `G = SL₂ × GL_{g+6}` of the smooth quasi-projective scheme `Fr_{g,n}` of framed covers, of `ℙ¹ × Fr_{g,n}` and of the universal curve over `Fr_{g,n}`. The parametrized Hurwitz space of covers of a fixed line (Canning–Larson genus seven, Remark 2.3, pp. 6–7) is not used: it is not a scheme where a cover has an automorphism over `ℙ¹`.

### From VirtualFundamentalClass

Consumed: §§2.1–2.4 (Vistoli's dimension-graded rational Chow groups `A_*(X)_ℚ` of separated Deligne–Mumford stacks over a field, with `A^c(X)_ℚ = A_{n−c}(X)_ℚ` for `X` pure of dimension `n` and the stabilizer weights in degrees, the classifying stack of a finite group of order `m` having degree `1/m`; proper pushforward, flat pullback, localization and exterior products; homotopy invariance for vector bundles, Chern classes and the projective-bundle formula; refined Gysin maps along representable regular local immersions, with base change, the excess formula and compatibility with proper pushforward and flat pullback). Assumed: these are the rational Chow groups that StableReductionPartII consumes, so that `ψᵢ = c₁(Lᵢ)` for the lines of its §3.3 is a Chern class of §2.3, and the tautological rings of §0.1 are formed in them.

### From TauCetiRoadmap.AlgebraicCurves

Consumed: Layer 12, items 12B–12C (the regular projective model of a function field, smooth over a perfect field, and the anti-equivalence between function fields and integral regular projective curves, under which a subfield `K(f) ⊂ F` generated by a nonconstant function corresponds to a finite dominant morphism to `ℙ¹`, of degree the field degree `[F : K(f)]`). Assumed: over an algebraically closed field the gonality of the function field is therefore the least degree of a finite morphism from the curve to `ℙ¹`.

### From CanonicalModelsAndGonality

Consumed: §5.4 (Brill–Noether existence: for a function field `F` of genus `g` over an algebraically closed field and `ρ(g, r, d) = g − (r + 1)(g − d + r) ≥ 0`, a divisor of degree `d` with `ℓ(D) ≥ r + 1`, in every characteristic; for `r = 1` the bound `gon_{k̄}(F) ≤ ⌊(g + 3)/2⌋`); §§3.4 and 9.1 (Noether's theorem: over a perfect field, for genus at least three and a function field that is not geometrically hyperelliptic, the canonical system is very ample and the canonical morphism is a closed immersion onto the canonical model); §3.9(b) (in genus four, over an algebraically closed field, the homogeneous ideal of the canonical model is generated by an irreducible quadric, of rank three or four, and a cubic). Assumed: the bound is an upper bound only; hyperelliptic curves have gonality two in every genus; these are statements on function fields with exact constants, carried to curves by AlgebraicCurves Layer 12.

Assumed: these are the stable graphs of the boundary strata of `M̄_{g,n}`, legs recording the markings.

Consumed: §0.4 (the stable and smooth pointed moduli groupoids `M̄_{g,n}` and `M_{g,n}` realized on all schemes, morphisms preserving each ordered section); §2.248 (the universal curve over `M̄_{g,n}` identified with `M̄_{g,n+1}`, the old markings being sections); §§3.1–3.2 and 3.7 (separating and nonseparating clutching morphisms, finite and unramified); §3.3 (marking cotangent lines `Lᵢ = sᵢ^*ω` with `ψᵢ = c₁(Lᵢ)`); §§3.5 and 3.10 (stable one-edge boundary types, and the normalization of the boundary as the disjoint union of vertex-product quotient stacks by the constant graph-automorphism groups); §3.11 (the forgetful correction `Lᵢ = π^*Lᵢ ⊗ O(D_{i,n+1})`); §§4.1 and 4.14 (coarse algebraic spaces, proper over `ℤ`, and projectivity of the unpointed coarse space for `g ≥ 2` from a descended power of the determinant line); §§5.1, 5.10 and 5.11 (the Hodge bundle `E = f_*ω` of rank `g`, compatible with base change, its pullback under forgetting a marking and its separating-clutching splitting); §5.15 (for a smooth affine group `G` of dimension `d` acting properly on a quasi-projective `X` with a `G`-linearised ample invertible sheaf and `[X/G]` Deligne–Mumford, the isomorphism `A_{i−d}([X/G])_ℚ → A^G_i(X) ⊗ ℚ` from Vistoli's groups to the equivariant groups, by flat pullback to the mixed spaces); §7.11 (the export of the pointed stable stacks, relabelling, forgetful and clutching morphisms and the boundary normalization for tautological operations, which leaves the Chow and cohomological structures to this roadmap). What StableReductionPartII must provide (gap:pointed-coarse-projectivity): for every stable pair `(g, n)`, projectivity over `ℤ` of the coarse space of `M̄_{g,n}`, together with a rational combination of tautological divisor classes (for instance `κ₁ + Σψᵢ`) that is the pullback of an ample class on the coarse space.

### From WeilConjectures

Consumed: WC.1 (finite groupoid mass; stack points, compact supports and duality, including the compact-support trace formula `mass(X(k_r)) = Σ (−1)^i Tr(F^r | H_c^i)`, Künneth, finite group actions, the smooth proper Poincaré pairing, the stack Artin comparison and smooth proper transport; stack mass, quotients by connected groups via Lang's theorem and the separate torsor count for disconnected groups; twists and Frobenius; the integral character lattice; signed Frobenius configurations; inverse zeta and configuration coefficients; termination for proper even cohomology; the Hasse–Weil sieve); WC.5 (exact polynomial point count; the local cutoff for approximate polynomial counts; approximate counts and Tate semisimplification); WC.6 (smooth proper DM stack purity). Assumed: each DM application carries the stack trace, comparison, purity and duality inputs explicitly, as WC.5 states. The clauses of WC.5 on polynomial counts over all of `Spec ℤ` and on equivariant polynomial counts are not applied to stacks: their Deligne–Mumford versions rest on the descent of the crystalline and de Rham comparison to smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stacks over a `p`-adic trait, which WC.1 (stack points, compact supports and duality) provides and this roadmap does not consume. For stacks this roadmap uses from WC.5 only the exact-count definition, the local cutoff and the approximate-count theorem, whose conclusions are dimensions, Frobenius eigenvalues and semisimplifications; the actual Tate structure of `H^*(M̄_{4,n})` comes from tautological generation (§9.7), and representation-valued counts come from the trace identity of §1.5.

What WeilConjectures must provide (gap:open-dm-stack-duality): for a smooth separated finite-type Deligne–Mumford stack `U` of pure dimension `d`, not necessarily proper, over an algebraically closed field or a finite field with `ℓ` invertible, the Frobenius- and Galois-equivariant perfect pairing `H^k(U) ⊗ H_c^{2d−k}(U) → ℚ_ℓ(−d)`, compatible with finite group actions, Künneth, open restriction and proper pushforward, extending the smooth proper pairing of WC.1.

Assumed: §8.24 is stated over finite fields; its use in the `G_ℚ`-realization goes through the stack Artin comparison and smooth proper transport of WeilConjectures, WC.1, as in the Conventions.

What DeligneWeightsAndPurity must provide (gap:dm-stack-hard-lefschetz): ℓ-adic hard Lefschetz for a smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stack of pure dimension `d` with projective coarse space: for the pullback `a` of an ample class, cup product with `a^{d−k}` is an isomorphism `H^k → H^{2d−k}(d−k)` for `0 ≤ k ≤ d`.

### From SchemeKTheoryOperations

Consumed: §§6.2.1–6.2.2 (pre-λ-rings, special λ-rings, universal identities and Adams operations), as the framework in which the completed symmetric functions of §1.4 carry their Adams operations.

### From HabiroNahmSeries

Consumed: §5.1 (augmentation-complete multivariate logarithm and exponential over a `ℚ`-algebra, with inverse laws and coefficient formulas).

What HabiroNahmSeries must provide (gap:complete-adams-series): for a commutative `ℚ`-algebra `R` with a special λ-structure, complete and separated for a decreasing multiplicative filtration `F^•` (`F^a F^b ⊂ F^{a+b}`), whose Adams operations `ψ_r` are commuting continuous ring endomorphisms with `ψ_r(F^a) ⊂ F^{ra}` for all `a ≥ 1` and `r ≥ 1`, the plethystic exponential `Exp(x) = exp(Σ_{r≥1} ψ_r(x)/r)` and logarithm `Log(1 + y) = Σ_{r≥1} (μ(r)/r) ψ_r(log(1 + y))`, as mutually inverse bijections `F^1 → 1 + F^1` with `Exp(x + y) = Exp(x) Exp(y)`, extending the augmentation-complete calculus of §5.1, where augmentation variables satisfy `ψ_r(t_i) = t_i^r`. The scaling condition is what makes `Σ ψ_r(x)/r` converge; continuity alone does not. The completed symmetric functions of §1.4 are such an algebra, `ψ_r` multiplying the degree (`deg p_i = i`, `deg h = 2`) by `r`.

Assumed: no statement on cohomology; the comparison `H^*(M_{g,n}, ℚ) ≅ H^*(Mod_{g,n}, ℚ)` is §2.1.


### From PELModuli

Consumed: §M5.2 (the Siegel moduli `A_{g,D,n}`, at `g = 2`, principal type and `n = 1`: the smooth separated algebraic stack `A₂` of principally polarized abelian surfaces over `ℤ`, of relative dimension three, with its universal family and the stabilizers it retains at level one). Assumed: the standard local system `R¹` of the universal family and its symplectic Schur functors `V_{a,b}` are formed on this stack, as in §3.6.

## How to read the build

Layer 0 sets up the tautological rings, kappa classes, the stable-pair order, the boundary exact sequences and the cusp realizations, and identifies the rational Chow theories of stacks that the later layers use. Layer 1 builds the equivariant counting machinery on curve moduli (marking twists, the weight-graded equivariant Euler characteristic, the stable-graph transform and the Getzler–Kapranov formula) and the low-genus polynomial counts. Layer 2 proves the dimension bounds and odd vanishing through degree seven and, for genus at most three, through degree nine. Layer 3 treats genus one, symplectic local systems and the stable range, and computes the cusp multiplicities of genus-one and genus-two moduli beyond the polynomial range that the graphical bases of Layer 11 use. Layer 4 constructs semi-tautological extensions and proves finite generation. Layer 5 develops the Chow–Künneth generation property and its known ranges in genus at most four and on the hyperelliptic and trigonal loci (§5.4). Layers 6 and 7 establish it for `M_{7,n}`, `n ≤ 3`, through its gonality strata, and Layer 6 also gives the ranges in genera five and six (§6.9) from the tetragonal Hurwitz stack and the Casnati–Ekedahl pushforwards (§§6.3, 6.5). Layers 8 and 9 compute the hyperelliptic and genus-four point counts and prove odd vanishing through degree nine in all genera; they are independent of Layers 4–7, except that §9.7 uses the Chow–Künneth ranges of §5.4. Layer 10 treats even cohomology and homology and, through the oriented boundary complex, proves that `H¹¹` vanishes outside genus one; Layer 11 the exceptional genus-one and genus-two cusp bases, and Layer 12 proves the generation and classification theorems in degrees thirteen to fifteen. Each target's *Needs* line lists the inputs of its statement and of the route given; another route may need less.

## Layer 0: Tautological operations and cusp realizations

This layer adds to the imported moduli stacks the ring operations and realization conventions specific to curve cohomology. The test of every pushforward is its codimension and its Tate twist.

### 0.1 Tautological Chow and cohomology rings

Define the tautological system as the least collection of unital graded `ℚ`-subalgebras `R^*(M̄_{g,n}) ⊂ A^*(M̄_{g,n})`, one for each stable pair, that is closed under pushforward along forgetful and clutching morphisms, under pullback along forgetful and clutching morphisms, and under relabelling of markings. The full Chow system satisfies these conditions, and an intersection of such systems satisfies them, so the least system exists. Put `R^*(M_{g,n})` for the image of `R^*(M̄_{g,n})` under restriction, and `RH^*` for the image of `R^*` under the cycle class map; cohomological degree is twice Chow codimension. Prove that `R^*(M̄_{g,n})` is spanned by pushforwards from boundary strata of monomials in marking psi classes and vertex kappa classes (the decorated stable-graph description), by the projection formula, the excess intersection formula for clutching pullbacks and the forgetful pullback formulas for psi and kappa classes (CLP, Introduction pp. 1–3 and §2.1 p. 4; the graph description is recalled in CLP §5, pp. 13–22).

*Needs:* StableReductionPartII §§3.1–3.3 and 7.11 (forgetful and clutching morphisms, relabelling, cotangent lines); VirtualFundamentalClass §§2.1–2.4 (Vistoli's rational Chow groups of the separated Deligne–Mumford stacks `M̄_{g,n}`, proper pushforward, flat pullback, exterior products and Chern classes, and refined Gysin maps along representable regular local immersions with the excess formula and compatibility with proper pushforward, which give the intersection product `x · y = Δ^!(x × y)` along the diagonal, pullback along clutching morphisms through their graphs, and the projection formula); MotivesAndAlgebraicCycles, gap:dm-stack-cycle-class (the cycle class map for `RH^*`).

API:

- Every collection of unital graded subalgebras closed under the tautological operations contains the tautological system.
- Restriction maps `R^*(M̄_{g,n})` onto `R^*(M_{g,n})`, by definition.
- The cycle class map sends `R^c` into `RH^{2c}` with Hodge type `(c, c)` and commutes with the tautological operations.
- `R^*(M̄_{g,n})` is spanned by decorated stable-graph pushforwards of psi and kappa monomials.
- Two operation-stable systems with equal subalgebras at every stable pair are equal.

**Checks.**

- `R^*(M̄_{0,3}) = ℚ` in degree zero only, since `M̄_{0,3}` is a point; a definition that also adjoined a nonzero degree-one class would be wrong here.
- On `M̄_{0,4} ≅ ℙ¹` a boundary point is a nonzero tautological class in `R¹` and in `RH²`; the three boundary points have equal classes.
- A nonzero class in `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` is not in `RH^*`: tautological classes are algebraic and have even degree. A definition of `RH^*` as "all cohomology" would contain it.

### 0.2 Kappa classes of pointed curves

For a stable pair `(g, n)` and the forgetful morphism `π : M̄_{g,n+1} → M̄_{g,n}` define `κ_a = π_*(ψ_{n+1}^{a+1}) ∈ R^a(M̄_{g,n})` for `a ≥ 0`. The marking line and its psi class are StableReductionPartII §3.3; `λ_j = c_j(E)` for the Hodge bundle `E` of StableReductionPartII §5.1; no new Hodge bundle is constructed. Prove the comparison `κ_a = π^*κ_a + ψ_{n+1}^a` on `M̄_{g,n+1}` (CLP, §2.1 p. 4; §§3 and 5, pp. 9–10 and 21–22).

*Needs:* §0.1; StableReductionPartII §§3.3 and 3.11 (cotangent lines and their forgetful correction) and §5.1 (Hodge bundle); VirtualFundamentalClass §§2.2–2.3 (proper pushforward and Chern classes on Deligne–Mumford stacks).

API:

- `κ_a` has Chow codimension `a`.
- On `M̄_{g,n+1}`, `κ_a = π^*κ_a + ψ_{n+1}^a`, where the left side is the kappa class of `(g, n+1)` and `π^*κ_a` the pullback of that of `(g, n)`.
- `κ₀ = (2g − 2 + n)·1`.
- The lambda classes are the Chern classes of the imported finite locally free Hodge bundle and satisfy its determinant and exterior-power identities.
- For every `a ≥ 0`, `π_*(ψ_{n+1}^{a+1}) = κ_a`, pushforward lowering Chow degree by one.

**Checks.**

- `κ₀ = 1` on `M̄_{0,3}` (`2·0 − 2 + 3 = 1`).
- `κ₀ = 1` on `M̄_{1,1}` (`2 − 2 + 1 = 1`).
- `κ₀` increases by one when a marking is added; the unpointed value `2g − 2` would be wrong for `n > 0`. On `M̄_7`, `κ₀ = 12`, the value used in §7.7.

### 0.3 The stable-pair order

For pairs of natural numbers define `(g′, n′) ≺ (g, n)` if and only if `g′ ≤ g`, `2g′ + n′ ≤ 2g + n` and the pairs are different. Prove that `≺` is a well-founded strict partial order, by lexicographic descent of `(g, 2g + n)`, that every vertex pair `(g_v, n_v)` of a stable graph with at least one edge and total pair `(g, n)` satisfies `(g_v, n_v) ≺ (g, n)`, and that `(g, n − 1) ≺ (g, n)` (CLP, §2.1 p. 4; BFP, Notation 4.1, p. 1331).

*Needs:* StableReduction, Layer 1 (dual graphs and their genus formula), implemented as `TauCeti.DualGraph` and `TauCeti.DualGraph.arithmeticGenus`.

API:

- No pair precedes itself.
- The relation is transitive and well founded.
- Every vertex of a stable graph with at least one edge precedes its total pair.
- `(g, n − 1)` precedes `(g, n)` when both are stable and `n > 0`.

**Checks.**

- `(0, 4) ≺ (1, 2)`, although both have `2g + n = 4`.
- `(1, 2)` does not precede `(0, 4)`, since its genus is larger.
- `(1, 1)` does not precede itself; the unstable pair `(0, 2)` precedes `(0, 3)` numerically but is excluded from every moduli statement.

### 0.4 Pure-weight boundary exact sequences

Let `(g, n)` be stable and let `ν : D̃ → M̄_{g,n}` be the normalization of the boundary, a disjoint union of quotients of products of compact moduli stacks by graph automorphisms. Prove the exact sequences

    H^{k−2}(D̃)(−1) → H^k(M̄_{g,n}) → W_k H^k(M_{g,n}) → 0,
    H_k(D̃) → H_k(M̄_{g,n}) → W_{−k} H_k^{BM}(M_{g,n}) → 0,

in both realizations, where the first map is the sum of the Gysin maps of the normalized one-edge strata, taken on graph-automorphism invariants, and `H_k^{BM}(M_{g,n}) = H^{2d−k}(M_{g,n})(d)` (CLP, §2.1 p. 4; §6.2, equation (6.1), p. 23). Route: the Gysin sequence of the pair `(M̄_{g,n}, ∂M̄_{g,n})`, strictness of the weight filtration and purity of the smooth proper strata identify the image of the boundary with the image of the normalized one-edge Gysin maps; dualize with the Tate twist.

*Needs:* SchemeAndStackFoundations §4.15 (normal-crossings boundary); StableReductionPartII §3.10 (normalization of the boundary with graph automorphisms); DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 (weight spectral sequence and purity, over finite fields) with WeilConjectures, WC.1 (stack Artin comparison and smooth proper transport, for the `G_ℚ`-realization, as in the Conventions); MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (the Hodge analogue); WeilConjectures, gap:open-dm-stack-duality (duality on `M_{g,n}`); §0.3.

**Checks.**

- For `M̄_{0,4}`, the Gysin map of a boundary point is `ℚ(−1) = H⁰(pt)(−1) → H²(ℙ¹)`, an isomorphism of weight two; omitting the twist `(−1)` would map a weight-zero space onto a weight-two space.
- `H_k` of a smooth proper `X` is `H^{2d−k}(X)(d)`, never `H^{2d−k}(X)` without the twist: for `ℙ¹`, `H₂ = H⁰(1)` has weight `−2`.

### 0.5 Cusp realizations on genus-one moduli

Prove that `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` and `W₁₅H¹⁵(M_{1,15})` are, in both realizations, the parabolic realizations of the level-one cusp forms of weights twelve and sixteen, each of rank two; that `S₁₂` has Hodge types `(11,0),(0,11)` and `S₁₆` has `(15,0),(0,15)`; and more generally that the sign-isotypic part of `W_k H^k(M_{1,k})` for the action of `S_k` on the markings is `S_{k+1}`, of rank `2 dim S_{k+1}(SL₂(ℤ))` (CLP, Introduction p. 1; Proposition 2.2, pp. 5–7; Lemma 8.3, p. 29; the genus-one classification is due to Getzler, recalled in CLP §2.2, p. 5). Route: the alternating part of the configuration space of `k − 1` points on the universal elliptic curve over `M_{1,1}` gives `Sym^{k−1}` of the standard local system, whose parabolic cohomology is the Eichler–Shimura realization.

*Needs:* StableReductionPartII §§0.4 and 2.248 (the stacks and universal curve); `CuspForm`, `CuspForm.rank_eq_one_of_weight_eq_twelve`, `CuspForm.discriminantEquiv`, `ModularForm.dimension_level_one`; `TauCeti.finrank_periodPolynomials_eq_finrank_modularForm_add_finrank_cuspForm`; AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations §1.2 (parabolic realizations at level one); MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (Hodge structure of the stacks); the symmetric powers of the standard local system `R¹f_*ℚ` on `M_{1,1}`, which on this one-dimensional base need no input beyond the universal elliptic curve.

**Checks.**

- `S₁₄ = 0`, while `S₁₂` and `S₁₆` each have rational dimension two, from `dim S₁₄ = 0`, `dim S₁₂ = dim S₁₆ = 1`.
- `L S₁₂` has pure weight thirteen and Hodge types `(12,1),(1,12)`, not `(11,0),(0,11)`: the twist shifts both Hodge numbers by one.
- `S_r` has rank `2 dim S_r(SL₂(ℤ))`, not always two: for weight twenty-four the cusp space has dimension two and `S₂₄` has rank four.

### 0.6 Comparison of the rational Chow theories of stacks

Let `k` be a field and `A_*(X)_ℚ` the rational Chow groups of a finite-type algebraic stack `X` over `k` stratified by quotient stacks (gap:stack-rational-chow), with the natural map from cycles sending an integral closed substack to its class. Prove the following (Kresch, Theorem 2.1.12 (ii)–(iii), p. 6, with their proofs on p. 8 and Remark 2.1.17, p. 8; stacks with quasi-finite diagonal are stratified by quotient stacks, Proposition 4.5.7, p. 28; the intersection product agrees with Edidin–Graham's on global quotients, §4.3, p. 24).

- For a separated Deligne–Mumford stack `X` of finite type over `k`, the natural map from Vistoli's rational Chow groups of VirtualFundamentalClass §2.1 to `A_*(X)_ℚ` is an isomorphism in every dimension, compatible with flat pullback, pushforward along projective morphisms, exterior products and Chern classes of vector bundles, and for smooth `X` it is an isomorphism of rings.
- For `G` smooth affine over `k` and `X` quasi-projective over `k` with a `G`-linearised ample invertible sheaf, the natural map `A^G_i(X) ⊗ ℚ → A_{i − dim G}([X/G])_ℚ` from the equivariant Chow groups of EquivariantIntersectionTheory §2.1, graded so that `[X]` lies in `A^G_{dim X}(X)`, is an isomorphism for every `i`, with the same compatibilities, and for smooth `X` an isomorphism of rings in the codimension grading.
- When `G` acts properly and `[X/G]` is Deligne–Mumford, the inverse of the second isomorphism composed with the first is the comparison of StableReductionPartII §5.15.

Consequently the tautological rings of §0.1 are subrings of the rational Chow rings of gap:stack-rational-chow, and `A^*(BGL_n)_ℚ = ℚ[c₁, …, c_n]` by EquivariantIntersectionTheory §2.3. Route: a class of the gap's groups is represented, after pullback to the total space of a vector bundle, by a cycle; on a Deligne–Mumford stack pullback to a vector bundle is an isomorphism on Vistoli's rational groups (VirtualFundamentalClass §2.3), and on a quotient stack every vector bundle is dominated by the bundles of representations of `G` acting freely off a closed subset of high codimension, which are the approximations of EquivariantIntersectionTheory §2.1.

*Needs:* SchemeAndStackFoundations, gap:stack-rational-chow; VirtualFundamentalClass §§2.1–2.3; EquivariantIntersectionTheory §§2.1–2.3; StableReductionPartII §5.15.

**Checks.**

- The comparison is rational only. For `k` of characteristic not two, `BC₂ = [Spec k/μ₂]` is Deligne–Mumford and a quotient stack; `A^*_{μ₂} = ℤ[t]/(2t)` (EquivariantIntersectionTheory §2.3) is `ℤ/2` in every positive codimension, while the integral cycles of the zero-dimensional `BC₂` are `ℤ` in dimension zero; after `⊗ ℚ` both sides are `ℚ` in codimension zero and vanish elsewhere.
- The shift by `dim G` is forced. For `𝔾_m` acting on `X = 𝔸² ∖ 0` with weights `1`, `[X/𝔾_m] = ℙ¹`, and the fundamental class `[X] ∈ A^{𝔾_m}_2(X)` goes to `[ℙ¹] ∈ A_1(ℙ¹)_ℚ = ℚ`; without the shift it would land in `A_2(ℙ¹)_ℚ = 0`.
- The first item needs the Deligne–Mumford hypothesis. `B𝔾_m` is a quotient stack, and the second item gives `A^*(B𝔾_m)_ℚ = ℚ[t]`, nonzero in every codimension; its only integral closed substack is itself and its rational functions are constant, so its cycles modulo rational equivalence are `ℚ` in dimension `−1` alone.

### Examples

On `M̄_{0,4} = ℙ¹` the tautological ring is `ℚ[δ]/(δ²)` with `δ` the class of a boundary point; its cohomological realization is all of `H^*(ℙ¹)`. On `M̄_{1,1}`, `κ₀ = 1` and `ψ₁ = δ_irr/12` with the stack normalization of the boundary divisor. The first non-tautological cohomology of any `M̄_{g,n}` is `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11}) = S₁₂`.

### Dependencies

StableReductionPartII §§0.4, 2.248, 3.1–3.3, 3.10–3.11, 5.1, 5.15, 7.11; StableReduction, Layer 1; SchemeAndStackFoundations §§1.18–1.23, 4.15 and Layer 5 with gap:stack-rational-chow; VirtualFundamentalClass §§2.1–2.4; EquivariantIntersectionTheory §§2.1–2.3; DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory with gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge; WeilConjectures, WC.1, with gap:open-dm-stack-duality; MotivesAndAlgebraicCycles with gap:dm-stack-cycle-class; AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations §1.2; Mathlib level-one modular forms; Tau Ceti level-one period polynomials.

## Layer 1: Equivariant counts and the stable-graph transform

Twisting the markings by a permutation extracts symmetric-group characters from point counts; summing over stable graphs assembles boundary counts from the open strata. Two different sign conventions occur and are kept apart: the ordinary assembly of open strata uses the Koszul signs of graph automorphisms only, while the weight spectral sequence of normalized closed strata carries the edge-orientation character `det ℚ^{E(Γ)}` (DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24).

### 1.1 Twists of moduli by permutations of the markings

For a stable pair `(g, n)`, a finite field `𝔽_q` and `σ ∈ S_n`, define the twist `M_{g,n}^σ` over `𝔽_q` as the descent of `M_{g,n} ⊗ 𝔽̄_q` along the twisted Frobenius `σF_q`: its rational points over `𝔽_q` are smooth curves over `𝔽_q` with `n` distinct geometric marked points permuted by Frobenius as `σ` permutes the labels. Define `M̄_{g,n}^σ`, `∂M̄_{g,n}^σ` and the hyperelliptic twist `H_{g,n}^σ` in the same way. Prove that the twist exists as a DM stack of finite type over `𝔽_q`, that its groupoid mass equals the alternating trace of `F_q σ^*` on `H_c^*(M_{g,n} ⊗ 𝔽̄_q, ℚ_ℓ)`, and that the twists are compatible with forgetting a union of Frobenius cycles (BFP, §9.1, pp. 1351–1352, and §11, pp. 1356–1357). The twist construction for schemes and its trace formula are WeilConjectures, WC.1 (twists and Frobenius); this target extends them to the curve moduli stacks, where descent must retain automorphism groups.

*Needs:* StableReductionPartII §§0.4 and 7.11 (the stacks, relabelling and forgetful morphisms); WeilConjectures, WC.1 (finite groupoid mass, stack compact supports and trace, twists and Frobenius); SchemeAndStackFoundations §§1.18–1.21 (effective descent for stacks).

API:

- Conjugate permutations give isomorphic twists with equal masses; conjugating `σ` by a marking permutation gives an isomorphism of twists compatible with compact-support traces.
- The mass of `M_{g,n}^σ(𝔽_q)` is `Σ_i (−1)^i Tr(F_q σ^* | H_c^i(M_{g,n} ⊗ 𝔽̄_q, ℚ_ℓ))`.
- The identity twist is the ordinary moduli stack with ordered rational markings.
- Forgetting a union of Frobenius cycles descends the corresponding forgetful morphism.

**Checks.**

- For `σ` of cycle type `(1,1)` the rational points have two distinct rational marks; for type `(2)` they have one Frobenius orbit of two distinct geometric points, and no rational marked point.
- A fixed mark together with a conjugate pair has cycle type `(2,1)`, not three independent rational marks. On `ℙ¹` over `𝔽_q`, the twisted configuration count for cycle type `(2)` is `q² − q` (one degree-two point with its two labellings), while for type `(1,1)` it is `(q + 1)q`; a definition that ignored the twist would return `(q + 1)q` in both cases.
- The groupoid of rational points is not replaced by its set of isomorphism classes: for `M_{1,1}` over `𝔽_q` the mass is `q`, while the number of isomorphism classes is `2q + O(1)` (twists of each `j`-invariant).

### 1.2 The weight-graded equivariant Euler characteristic

For a finite group `G`, a separated finite-type DM stack `X` over `ℂ` with a `G`-action, define `e_c^G(X) = Σ_{i,w} (−1)^i [Gr^W_w H_c^i(X)]` in the Grothendieck group of rational mixed Hodge structures with `G`-action; in the `ℓ`-adic realization over a finite field use the Frobenius weights. When every contributing weight piece is a sum of Tate structures, write `e_c^G(X) = Σ_j a_j L^j` with `a_j` in the rational representation ring of `G`; for `G = S_n` identify `a_j` with its Frobenius characteristic, a degree-`n` symmetric function (BFP, §9, pp. 1351–1354, and Remark 1.6, p. 1326). No positivity or purity of individual groups of an open stack is asserted.

*Needs:* `TauCeti.repRing`, `TauCeti.repRingCharacter`; SchurWeyl, Layer 7 (fixed-degree Frobenius characteristic); DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 (Frobenius weights over finite fields); MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (weights, Hodge); WeilConjectures, WC.1 (Künneth and localization for compact supports).

API:

- For a closed `G`-stable substack `Z ⊂ X` with complement `U`, `e_c^G(X) = e_c^G(U) + e_c^G(Z)`.
- `e_c` of a product is the tensor product, with Koszul signs in cohomological degree.
- Evaluating characters at the identity recovers the nonequivariant weight Euler polynomial.
- Under the trace hypotheses of WeilConjectures, WC.1 and WC.5, replacing `L` by `q` and the Adams operation by `ψ_r(q) = q^r` gives the twisted point counts of §1.1.
- The coefficient of weight `w` is the alternating sum of the `Gr^W_w H_c^i` as virtual representations; it is not ordinary `H^i` for a nonproper stack.

**Checks.**

- `e_c(𝔸¹) = L`, since `H_c²(𝔸¹) = ℚ(−1)` is the only compactly supported group.
- `e_c(𝔾_m) = L − 1`: coefficients need not be nonnegative.
- For `S₃`, the standard representation coefficient evaluated at the identity contributes twice its multiplicity: `e_c^{S₃} = s_{(2,1)}` has nonequivariant value `2`.

### 1.3 The stable-graph transform

For a stable collection `V = (V(g, n))` of finite-dimensional graded rational `S_n`-representations, zero when `2g − 2 + n ≤ 0`, define `MV(g, n) = ⊕_Γ (⊗_v V(g_v, n_v))_{Aut Γ}`, the sum over isomorphism classes of connected stable graphs `Γ` of total genus `g` with `n` legs, where `Aut Γ` acts by permuting flags with Koszul signs of the cohomological grading and the sum is finite for each `(g, n)` (GK, §2, the free modular operad; recalled in GK §8 and in BFP, proof of Proposition 9.6, p. 1354). There is no edge-orientation twist in this ordinary transform. In characteristic zero coinvariants equal invariants.

*Needs:* StableReduction, Layer 1 (dual graphs and their genus formula, `TauCeti.DualGraph`); StableReductionPartII §3.10 (graph automorphisms of boundary strata, acting on flags); §0.3; SchurWeyl, Layer 7.

API:

- The graph with one vertex and no edge contributes `V(g, n)`.
- `MV − V` is exactly the sum over connected stable graphs with at least one edge.
- Relabelling the legs defines the `S_n`-action on `MV(g, n)`.
- The total genus of `Γ` is `Σ_v g_v + b₁(Γ)`.
- A morphism of stable collections from `V` to the underlying collection of a modular operad extends uniquely to a morphism of modular operads from `MV`, compatible with flag permutations and contractions.

**Checks.**

- A one-vertex graph with one loop raises the genus by one and uses two flags of the vertex: from `V(g − 1, n + 2)` it contributes to `MV(g, n)`.
- A separating one-edge graph has total genus `g₁ + g₂`.
- For a two-edge graph with an automorphism exchanging the edges, the ordinary transform takes plain invariants; inserting the sign of the edge permutation would give the term of the closed-strata weight complex instead (compare §12.6).

### 1.4 The Getzler–Kapranov formula

In the completed ring of SchurWeyl, gap:completed-symmetric-functions, put `Ch(V) = Σ_{g,n} h^{g−1} ch(V(g, n))` and `Δ = Σ_{r≥1} h^r ((r/2) ∂²/∂p_r² + ∂/∂p_{2r})`. Prove `Ch(MV) = Log(exp(Δ) Exp(Ch(V)))`, where `exp(Δ)` is the ordinary exponential of the operator and `Exp`, `Log` are the plethystic exponential and logarithm (GK, Theorem 8.13; BFP, proof of Proposition 9.6, p. 1354, which applies it; the opening of BFP §9, p. 1351, cites it as Theorem 8.3). Route: `Exp` forms unordered collections of vertices, `exp(Δ)` joins pairs of flags (the factor `r/2` counts edges joining two flags in an `r`-cycle and the `p_{2r}` term edges whose endpoints are exchanged), and `Log` selects connected graphs; Euler's formula gives the exponent of `h`. For point counts specialize `ψ_r(q) = q^r`.

*Needs:* §1.3; §1.2; SchurWeyl, gap:completed-symmetric-functions; SchemeKTheoryOperations §§6.2.1–6.2.2; HabiroNahmSeries §5.1 with gap:complete-adams-series.

**Checks.**

- `Δ(p₁²) = h·(1/2)·2 = h` and `Δ(p₂) = h`: both terms of `Δ` are pinned by these two values.
- The positive-edge part `Ch(MV) − Ch(V)` vanishes in genus zero with three legs: `M̄_{0,3}` has no boundary.
- `Exp` needs Adams operations that scale the filtration (`ψ_r(F^a) ⊂ F^{ra}`, gap:complete-adams-series). On `ℚ[[t]]` with `ψ_r(t) = t^r`, `Σ_r ψ_r(t)/r = Σ_r t^r/r`, so `Exp(t) = (1 − t)^{−1}` and `Log((1 − t)^{−1}) = t`. With the binomial λ-structure, whose Adams operations are all the identity, the partial sums of `Σ_r ψ_r(t)/r` modulo `t²` are `t, 3t/2, 11t/6, …` and do not converge; `ψ₂(t) = t ∉ F²` excludes it.
- On the collection `V(0, 3) = ℚ` (trivial) and zero otherwise, `MV(0, 4)` is three-dimensional with the permutation representation of `S₄` on the three partitions of four labels into pairs, matching the three boundary points of `M̄_{0,4}`.

### 1.5 Polynomial counts of open moduli and of the boundary

Let `(g, n)` be stable and suppose `M̄_{g′,n′}` has polynomial point count for every `(g′, n′) ≼ (g, n)`. Prove that every Frobenius eigenvalue on `H_c^*(M_{g,n} ⊗ 𝔽̄_q, ℚ_ℓ)` is an integral power of `q`, that `M_{g,n}` and `∂M̄_{g,n}` have polynomial point counts, and that their weight-graded equivariant Euler characteristics determine all twisted counts (BFP, Proposition 4.2, pp. 1331–1332; Corollary 9.5, p. 1353). Route: the weight spectral sequence of normalized closed strata with orientation characters controls the eigenvalues; ordinary graph additivity of open strata (§§1.3–1.4) counts the boundary. Frobenius on `H_c^*` of the open stack is not asserted to be semisimple, and no semisimplicity is needed for the twisted counts: if `F` acts on a finite-dimensional space with every eigenvalue equal to `q^j` and commutes with an operator `σ` of finite order, then `F = q^j U` with `U` unipotent commuting with `σ`, `U` preserves the eigenspaces of `σ` and is unipotent on each, and `tr(σF) = q^j tr(σ)`; applied to the weight-graded pieces of `H_c^*`, this expresses each twisted count through the weight-graded equivariant Euler characteristic.

*Needs:* §§1.1–1.4; §0.3; DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24; WeilConjectures, WC.5 (the local cutoff, which for an exact count gives the dimensions and Frobenius eigenvalues of each compact stratum) and WC.6 (smooth proper DM purity).

**Checks.**

- The orientation character appears in the weight spectral sequence but not in the open-stratum sum: for `M̄_{1,2}`, the codimension-two stratum of two rational vertices joined by two edges, each carrying one leg, has an edge-exchanging automorphism acting by `−1` on `det ℚ^E`, so its twisted `H⁰` invariants vanish while its untwisted invariants do not.
- A class function given by arbitrary twisted counts need not lie in the integral representation ring: for `X = Spec 𝔽_{q³}` with `G = C₃`, the twisted counts are `(0, 0, 3)` up to ordering, which is not a rational character (BFP, Definition 9.1, p. 1351, read with this qualification; WeilConjectures, WC.1, the integral character lattice).
- The trace identity needs commutation, not semisimplicity. With `U` the unipotent matrix with rows `(1, 1, 0)`, `(0, 1, 0)`, `(0, 0, 1)` and `F = q^j U`, the involution `σ = diag(1, 1, −1)` commutes with `F` and `tr(σF) = q^j(1 + 1 − 1) = q^j = q^j tr(σ)`, although `F` is not semisimple; the transposition `σ′` exchanging the first two basis vectors does not commute with `F`, and `tr(σ′F) = q^j(0 + 1 + 1) = 2q^j ≠ q^j tr(σ′) = q^j`.

### 1.6 Polynomial point counts in low genus

Prove that `M̄_{g,n}` has polynomial point count over every finite field, with `S_n`-equivariant polynomial counts, for `g = 0` and all `n`, for `g = 1` and `n ≤ 10`, for `g = 2` and `n ≤ 7`, and for `g = 3` and `n ≤ 5` (BFP, §2, p. 1329, recalling Getzler for genus one, Bergström's equivariant hyperelliptic counts §11.2 for genus two, and Bergström's genus-three counts Theorems 2.1–2.4; BFP, §9.3, proof of Proposition 9.6, p. 1354, for the equivariant ranges `g′ ≤ 3`, `(g′, n′) ≺ (4, 3)`). Route: count the open strata `M_{g,n}^σ` for every cycle type, through hyperelliptic equations in genus two and plane quartics together with hyperelliptic curves in genus three, and assemble the compact counts by §§1.4–1.5.

*Needs:* §§1.1–1.5 (with the trace identity of §1.5 for the equivariant counts); WeilConjectures, WC.1 (stack mass, quotients by connected groups via Lang) and WC.5 (exact polynomial point count and the local cutoff); StableReductionPartII §0.4.

**Checks.**

- The genus-one range stops at ten markings: `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11}) = S₁₂` has dimension two and its Frobenius traces `τ(p)` are not polynomial in `p`.
- `#M̄_{0,4}(𝔽_q) = q + 1` and `#M̄_{0,5}(𝔽_q) = q² + 5q + 1`.
- `#M_{1,1}(𝔽_q) = q` as a mass, while `#M̄_{1,1}(𝔽_q) = q + 1`.

### Examples

`e_c(M_{0,4}) = L − 2`, since `M_{0,4}` is `ℙ¹` minus three points; with the boundary of three points this gives `e_c(M̄_{0,4}) = L + 1`. As `S₄`-representations, the boundary contributes the three-dimensional permutation representation on pairings, which is `s_{(4)} + s_{(2,2)}`.

### Dependencies

Layer 0; StableReductionPartII §§0.4, 3.10, 7.11; StableReduction, Layer 1; SchemeAndStackFoundations §§1.18–1.21; WeilConjectures, WC.1, WC.5, WC.6; DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory with gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge; SchurWeyl, Layer 7 with gap:completed-symmetric-functions; SchemeKTheoryOperations §§6.2.1–6.2.2; HabiroNahmSeries §5.1 with gap:complete-adams-series; Tau Ceti representation rings.

## Layer 2: Dimension bounds and odd vanishing in low degree

Boundary induction turns dimension bounds into a finite list of base cases. The strict improvement for zero or one marking (§2.2) is essential: the virtual cohomological dimension alone leaves the base cases `H⁹(M̄₅)` and `H⁹(M̄_{5,1})` open (BFP, Remark 2.4, p. 1329).

### 2.1 The virtual cohomological dimension

Prove first the comparison: for a discrete group `Γ` acting properly discontinuously with finite stabilizers on a contractible complex manifold `T`, the rational Betti cohomology of the analytic stack `[T/Γ]`, the cohomology of its constant sheaf `ℚ`, which for `[T/Γ] = M_{g,n}^an` is the rational Betti cohomology of gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge, is `H^*(Γ, ℚ)`. Route: the action groupoid `Γ ⋉ T` is a proper étale presentation of `[T/Γ]` (Orbifolds O1, item 1), and the cohomology of the constant sheaf on the stack is computed on the étale atlas `T → [T/Γ]`, as on the scheme atlases of the gap, which gives the constant cohomology of this presentation. Orbifolds O4 identifies that cohomology with the rational singular cohomology of the classifying space `B(Γ ⋉ T)`, the fat realization of the nerve, and both with the rational cohomology of the coarse space `T/Γ` (items O4-N, O4-R and O4-S). The nerve of `Γ ⋉ T` is the simplicial manifold `T × Γ^•`, whose realization is the Borel construction `T ×_Γ EΓ` for the bar model of `EΓ`. Then `Γ` acts diagonally, freely and properly discontinuously on `T × EΓ`, which is contractible, and the quotient map `T × EΓ → T ×_Γ EΓ` is the quotient covering map of this action, so the rational cohomology of the Borel construction is `H^*(Γ, ℚ)` by `IsQuotientCoveringMap.singularCohomologyIsoGroupCohomology`. With the Teichmüller uniformization this gives `H^*(M_{g,n}, ℚ) ≅ H^*(Mod_{g,n}, ℚ)` for every stable pair, `Mod_{g,n}` the pure mapping class group.

Prove that `H^i(M_{g,n}, ℚ) = 0` for `i > 4g − 5` when `g ≥ 2` and `n = 0`, and for `i > 4g − 4 + n` when `g ≥ 1` and `n ≥ 1`. Route: by the comparison `H^*(M_{g,n}, ℚ) = H^*(Mod_{g,n}, ℚ)`; a torsion-free finite-index subgroup of the mapping class group has the stated cohomological dimension by Harer's equivariant spine of Teichmüller space, and the rational cohomology of the group is the invariants of that of the subgroup (Harer, the virtual cohomological dimension theorem; recalled in BFP, §2, p. 1328). These are rational bounds; they do not assert that the stacks have trivial stabilizers.

*Needs:* MappingClassGroupsAndCanonicalRepresentations §2.3 (Teichmüller uniformization); Orbifolds O1, item 1, and O4, items O4-N, O4-R and O4-S (the action-groupoid presentation and the comparison of its constant cohomology with its classifying space and coarse space); Tau Ceti `IsQuotientCoveringMap.singularCohomologyIsoGroupCohomology`; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (the Betti cohomology of the analytic stack); StableReductionPartII §0.4.

**Checks.**

- The comparison for `M_{1,1}`: `T` is the upper half-plane `ℍ` and `Γ = SL₂(ℤ)`, and the nerve `ℍ × SL₂(ℤ)^•` realizes to `ℍ ×_{SL₂(ℤ)} ESL₂(ℤ)`. Then `H¹(SL₂(ℤ), ℚ) = Hom(SL₂(ℤ), ℚ) = 0`, the abelianization being cyclic of order twelve, and `H^i(SL₂(ℤ), ℚ) = 0` for `i ≥ 2`, a free subgroup of finite index having cohomological dimension one; so both sides are `ℚ` in degree zero, as is the rational cohomology of the coarse space `𝔸¹`. Integrally the two differ: `H²(SL₂(ℤ), ℤ) = ℤ/12`.
- For `M_{1,1}` the bound is `4 − 4 + 1 = 1`. It allows a nonzero `H¹` and does not by itself give `H¹(M_{1,1}, ℚ) = 0`, which holds because `H¹(SL₂(ℤ), ℚ) = 0`.
- For `M₂` the bound is `3`; for `M_{g,1}` it is `4g − 3`, the two values used in §2.2.

### 2.2 Vanishing in the virtual cohomological dimension

Prove that `H^{4g−5}(M_g, ℚ) = 0` for `g ≥ 2` and `H^{4g−3}(M_{g,1}, ℚ) = 0` for `g ≥ 1` (for `g ≥ 2`: CFP, Theorem 1, which treats the closed surface and the surface with one marked point; MSS, Theorem 1.4 and Theorem 6.2, for the closed surface; recalled in BFP, proof of Proposition 2.1, p. 1328; both theorems are stated for `g ≥ 2`). For `g = 1` the statement is `H¹(M_{1,1}, ℚ) = H¹(SL₂(ℤ), ℚ) = Hom(SL₂(ℤ), ℚ) = 0` by the comparison of §2.1, the abelianization of `SL₂(ℤ)` being finite, cyclic of order twelve. No analogous statement for `n ≥ 2` is made: the top-weight part of `H_c^{n+2}(M_{2,n})` is nonzero for `4 ≤ n ≤ 17` (BFP, Remark 2.3, p. 1328).

*Needs:* §2.1; MappingClassGroupsAndCanonicalRepresentations §2.3.

**Checks.**

- For `g = 1` the marked statement is `H¹(M_{1,1}, ℚ) = H¹(SL₂(ℤ), ℚ) = 0`.
- With §2.2 the degree-nine base region of §2.5 contains no genus-five pair. With the bounds of §2.1 alone, the compact-support vanishing of §2.3 for `n ≤ 1` would hold only for `k < 2g − 1`, and `(5, 0)`, `(5, 1)` would enter the base region since `2·5 − 1 = 9` (BFP, Remark 2.4, p. 1329).

### 2.3 Low compact-support and Borel–Moore vanishing

For `g ≥ 1` prove that `H_c^k(M_{g,n}) = 0` if `k < 2g` and `n ∈ {0, 1}`, and if `k < 2g − 2 + n` and `n ≥ 2`; equivalently `H_k^{BM}(M_{g,n}) = 0` in the same ranges (BFP, Proposition 2.1, p. 1328, where the bound for `n ≥ 2` is credited to Arbarello–Cornalba; CLP §6.2, p. 23). Route: Poincaré duality `H_c^k ≅ H^{2d−k}(d)^∨` turns §§2.1–2.2 into these bounds.

*Needs:* §§2.1–2.2; WeilConjectures, gap:open-dm-stack-duality; WeilConjectures, WC.1 (stack Artin comparison, which carries the vanishing of §§2.1–2.2 to the `ℓ`-adic realization); MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge.

**Checks.**

- At `(g, n) = (4, 1)`, `d = 10` and the threshold is `2g = 8`. Through `H_c^k ≅ H^{20−k}(d)^∨`, the bound `4g − 4 + n = 13` of §2.1 alone gives `H_c^k = 0` only for `k < 20 − 13 = 7`; the case `k = 7` is `H¹³(M_{4,1}) = 0` of §2.2. A route without §2.2 would stop at `k < 7`. No statement is made at `k = 8`.
- At `(2, 12)` the threshold is `2·2 − 2 + 12 = 14`, which leaves the degree-fifteen calculation of §11.9.
- Genus zero is excluded: `M_{0,4}` is `ℙ¹` minus three points and has `H_c¹ ≅ ℚ²`.

### 2.4 The Arbarello–Cornalba boundary injection

Let `(g, n)` be stable with `H_c^k(M_{g,n}) = 0`. Prove that pullback to the normalization `D̃` of the boundary, the disjoint union of the normalized one-edge strata with their graph automorphisms, is injective on `H^k(M̄_{g,n})`, in both realizations (AC, Lemma (2.6); recalled in BFP, §2, p. 1328). Route: the exact sequence `H_c^k(M_{g,n}) → H^k(M̄_{g,n}) → H^k(∂M̄_{g,n})`, purity of `H^k(M̄_{g,n})`, and injectivity of the weight-`k` part of `H^k(∂M̄_{g,n})` into `H^k(D̃)`.

*Needs:* §0.4; DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge.

**Checks.**

- The hypothesis cannot be dropped: `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11}) ≠ 0` restricts to zero on every boundary component, whose factors have no `H¹¹`, and `H_c^{11}(M_{1,11}) ≠ 0`.
- Cohomology of the singular boundary is not replaced by that of its normalization in all weights; only the weight-`k` part injects.

### 2.5 The Arbarello–Cornalba odd-degree induction

Let `k ≥ 1` be odd and `h ≥ 0`. Suppose that `H^q(M̄_{0,n}) = 0` for every odd `q ≤ k` and every `n ≥ 3`, and that `H^q(M̄_{g,n}) = 0` for every odd `q ≤ k` and every stable pair with `1 ≤ g ≤ h` and `max(2g, 2g − 2 + n) ≤ q`. Then `H^q(M̄_{g,n}) = 0` for every odd `q ≤ k` and every stable pair with `g ≤ h`; if the second hypothesis holds for every `h`, the conclusion holds for every stable pair (AC, Lemma (2.9); BFP, §2, pp. 1328–1329). Route: induct on `q` and on the stable-pair order; outside the base region §2.3 gives `H_c^q(M_{g,n}) = 0`, so §2.4 injects `H^q` into the cohomology of boundary products, whose vertices have genus at most `g`, and in which Künneth produces an odd factor of smaller degree or smaller pair; genus-zero factors are covered by the first hypothesis.

*Needs:* §§2.3, 2.4; §0.3.

**Checks.**

- For `k = 9`, after the genus-at-most-three cases of §2.9, the base region leaves only `(4, n)` with `n ≤ 3`.
- The base region is finite for each `k`: `2g ≤ k` bounds the genus and `2g − 2 + n ≤ k` the markings.

### 2.6 Keel's theorem in genus zero

For `n ≥ 3` prove that the cycle class map `A^*(M̄_{0,n}) → H^{2*}(M̄_{0,n})` is a ring isomorphism, that the ring is generated by boundary divisors subject to the symmetry, four-point and disjointness relations, and that `H^{odd}(M̄_{0,n}) = 0` (Keel, Introduction, results (1) and (4), p. 545, and the list of results proved from the blow-up description, (1), p. 549, and (6), p. 550, for the cycle class isomorphism, the vanishing of odd homology and the divisor presentation with its three relations; BFP, §2, p. 1329; CLP §6.2, p. 23). Route: `M̄_{0,n+1}` is an iterated blow-up of `M̄_{0,n} × ℙ¹` along smooth centres (Keel, Theorem 1, p. 556); apply the blow-up formulas for Chow groups and cohomology. In particular `H^*(M̄_{0,n}) = RH^*(M̄_{0,n})`.

*Needs:* §0.1; SchemeAndStackFoundations Layer 5, §§5.1–5.7 (Chow groups of smooth projective schemes and the blow-up decomposition of §5.7); EtaleCohomologyFoundations §F.7 (the blow-up decomposition of `ℓ`-adic cohomology along a smooth centre); MotivesAndAlgebraicCycles §§2.6–2.7 (Betti and `ℓ`-adic cycle maps of smooth projective schemes, and their comparison).

**Checks.**

- `M̄_{0,3}` is a point; `M̄_{0,4} ≅ ℙ¹` has three equal boundary-point classes and `H² = ℚ`.
- `M̄_{0,5}` is the blow-up of `ℙ²` at four points: `b₂ = 5`, and its ten boundary divisors span a space of rank five.
- The open `M_{0,4}` has `H¹ ≠ 0`; the theorem concerns the compactification only.

### 2.7 Vanishing in degrees one, three and five

Prove that `H^k(M̄_{g,n}) = 0` for `k ∈ {1, 3, 5}` and every stable pair (AC, Theorem (2.1); BFP, proof of Theorem 1.1 for `k = 7`, p. 1329). Route: the base cases of §2.5 for `k ≤ 5` are the pairs with `g ≥ 1` and `max(2g, 2g − 2 + n) ≤ 5`, each with polynomial point count by §1.6; apply §2.5, with §2.6 in genus zero.

*Needs:* §§2.5, 2.6, 1.6; WeilConjectures, WC.5 (the local cutoff, applied to an exact count) and WC.6.

**Checks.**

- `H¹(M̄_{1,1}) = 0` although `M̄_{1,1}` has nontrivial stabilizers everywhere; rational coefficients kill stabilizer cohomology.
- The statement concerns compact moduli: `gr^W_4 H³(M_{1,n}) ≠ 0` for `n ≥ 4` (Petersen, Theorem 1.3), so `H³` of the open stack need not vanish.

### 2.8 Vanishing in degree seven

Prove that `H⁷(M̄_{g,n}) = 0` for every stable pair (BFP, Theorem 1.1 and its proof for `k = 7`, p. 1329). Route: the base cases with `g ≥ 1` and `max(2g, 2g − 2 + n) ≤ 7` lie in the ranges of §1.6, so their cohomology is even; apply §2.5 with §2.7, and with §2.6 in genus zero.

*Needs:* §§2.5, 2.6, 2.7, 1.6; WeilConjectures, WC.5 and WC.6.

**Checks.**

- No genus-four input is used: the base region for `k = 7` has `g ≤ 3`.
- Without §2.2 the base region for `k = 7` would include `M̄₄` and `M̄_{4,1}` (BFP, Remark 2.4, p. 1329).

### 2.9 Odd vanishing through nine in genus at most three

Prove that `H^q(M̄_{g,n}) = 0` for every odd `q ≤ 9` and every stable pair with `g ≤ 3` (BFP, §2, pp. 1328–1329). Route: apply §2.5 with `h = 3`, with §2.6 in genus zero; its degree-nine base cases have `n ≤ 9, 7, 5` in genera one, two, three, which are the ranges of §1.6.

*Needs:* §§2.5, 2.6, 1.6; WeilConjectures, WC.5.

**Checks.**

- No genus-four base case is supplied: `(4, 0)` has `2g = 8 ≤ 9` and is handled in Layer 9.
- At `(3, 5)`, `2g − 2 + n = 9` is the largest genus-three base case and lies in the polynomial range.

### Examples

For `k = 7` the base region consists of `(1, n)` with `n ≤ 7`, `(2, n)` with `n ≤ 5` and `(3, n)` with `n ≤ 3`, all with even cohomology. For `k = 9` it adds `(1, 8), (1, 9), (2, 6), (2, 7), (3, 4), (3, 5)` and `(4, n)` for `n ≤ 3`; the last four are the genus-four bases of Layer 9.

### Dependencies

Layers 0 and 1; MappingClassGroupsAndCanonicalRepresentations §2.3; Orbifolds O1 and O4; Tau Ceti `IsQuotientCoveringMap.singularCohomologyIsoGroupCohomology`; StableReductionPartII §§0.4, 3.10; SchemeAndStackFoundations Layer 5; EtaleCohomologyFoundations §F.7; WeilConjectures, WC.1, WC.5, WC.6, with gap:open-dm-stack-duality; DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory with gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge; MotivesAndAlgebraicCycles §§2.6–2.7.

## Layer 3: Genus one, symplectic local systems and the stable range

The genus-one cusp formula supplies the first non-tautological seeds. Symplectic local systems keep the tensor weight separate from the Specht representation that records the permutation of markings. Their Euler characteristics, assembled over stable graphs, give the cusp multiplicities beyond the polynomial range (§3.6).

### 3.1 Symplectic local systems on curve moduli

For `g ≥ 1` let `f : C → M_g` be the universal curve (for `g = 1`, over `M_{1,1}`) and `V = R¹f_*ℚ`, of rank `2g`, with the alternating cup-product pairing `V ⊗ V → ℚ(−1)`. For a partition `λ = (λ₁ ≥ … ≥ λ_g ≥ 0)` with at most `g` parts define `V_λ` as the summand of `V^{⊗|λ|}` cut out by the harmonic (contraction-free) tensors and the Young symmetrizer of `λ`, the irreducible local system of highest weight `λ` for the symplectic monodromy, of weight `|λ|` (CLP, §3, pp. 9–10; Petersen–Tavakol–Yin, §3.2; BFP, §11.4, p. 1362).
