Prove that the formation of `V_λ` commutes with pullback along morphisms of families of smooth curves, and that `V^{⊗n}` modulo the images of the `n(n−1)/2` insertions of the symplectic form is `⊕_{|λ|=n} V_λ ⊗ T_λ` when `S_n` permutes the tensor factors, and `⊕_{|λ|=n} V_λ ⊗ T_{λ^T}` when `σ ∈ S_n` acts by `sgn(σ)` times that permutation, the action on the `n`-fold tensor power of `V` placed in cohomological degree one with the Koszul sign rule, through which `S_n` acts on the Künneth summand `H¹ ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ H¹` of the fibre power `C^n` (Petersen–Tavakol–Yin, §3.2, pp. 10–11, and Remark 3.6, p. 14; SchurWeyl §9.7 for the two label conventions; CLP, proof of Lemma 3.1, p. 10, which uses the second action). The Young-symmetrizer definition of `V_λ` above uses the unsigned action.

*Needs:* StableReductionPartII §2.248 (the universal curve over `M_g`); SchurWeyl, Layers 2 and 3 (Young symmetrizers and Specht modules) and Layer 9 (Brauer contractions and harmonic tensors for the alternating form); DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 and MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (cohomology of local systems on DM stacks).

API:

- `V_{(1)} = V` and `V_∅ = ℚ`.
- `V_λ` is pure of weight `|λ|`.
- The central element `−1` of the symplectic group acts on `V_λ` by `(−1)^{|λ|}`; on `M₂`, where every curve is hyperelliptic, the hyperelliptic involution acts on the fibres of `V_λ` by this sign.
- Formation of `V_λ` commutes with pullback along morphisms of smooth curve families.
- The contraction `V ⊗ V → ℚ(−1)` is the cup product; the primitive part of `Λ²V` is `V_{(1,1)}`, the kernel of the contraction.

**Checks.**

- For `λ = (1)`, `V_λ = V` has rank `2g`, which is `8` on `M₄`.
- For `g = 1`, `V_{(r)} = Sym^r V`; there are no partitions with two nonzero parts.
- `Λ²V = V_{(1,1)} ⊕ ℚ(−1)`; identifying `V_{(1,1)}` with all of `Λ²V` would retain a contraction summand of rank one. For `g = 2`, `rank V_{(1,1)} = 6 − 1 = 5`.
- The two `S_n`-actions give transposed labels. For `g = 1` and `n = 2` the quotient of `V ⊗ V` by the symplectic class is `Sym²V = V_{(2)}`, of rank three. The unsigned transposition has trace `+3` on it, matching `V_{(2)} ⊗ T_{(2)}`; the signed transposition acts by `−1`, trace `−3`, matching `V_{(2)} ⊗ T_{(1,1)}`. Pairing `T_{λ^T}` with the unsigned action would give trace `−3` where the true value is `+3`.

### 3.2 Pure cohomology of smooth genus-one moduli

For `k ≥ 1` and `n ≥ k` prove that `W_k H^k(M_{1,n}) ≅ S_{k+1} ⊗ T_{(n−k+1, 1^{k−1})}` as `S_n`-equivariant Hodge structures and Galois representations, with a basis of the multiplicity space given by the `binom(n−1, k−1)` pullbacks from `W_k H^k(M_{1,A})` along the forgetful maps, `A` running over the `k`-element subsets of the markings containing `1`; and `W_k H^k(M_{1,n}) = 0` for `n < k` (CLP, Proposition 2.2, pp. 5–7, after Getzler). Route: `M_{1,n}` is open in the `(n−1)`-fold fibre power `E^{n−1}` of the universal elliptic curve over `M_{1,1}`, and the Leray decomposition and Künneth write `W_kH^k(E^{n−1})` as the sum of the groups `W_kH^p(M_{1,1}, R^{i_2}π_*ℚ ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ R^{i_n}π_*ℚ)`, `p + i_2 + ⋯ + i_n = k`, which surjects onto `W_kH^k(M_{1,n})`. Summands with some `i_s = 2` are pushed forward from the loci where the point `s` meets the origin and die on `M_{1,n}`; summands with some `i_s = 0` are pulled back along the map forgetting `s`; and `H^p(M_{1,1}, −) = 0` rationally for `p ≥ 2`, the group `SL₂(ℤ)` having virtual cohomological dimension one. This leaves the groups `W_kH^p(M_{1,1}, V^{⊗q})`, `p ≤ 1`, `p + q = k`, one for each choice of the `q` factors with `i_s = 1`. By §3.1 with `g = 1`, `V^{⊗q}` is the sum of `Sym^qV` and of the images of the insertions of the symplectic class `θ_{st}`, the `R¹ ⊗ R¹` Künneth component of the class of the diagonal where the points `s` and `t` meet; the diagonal and the two loci where `s` or `t` meets the origin lie in the complement of `M_{1,n}`, so `θ_{st}` and its products restrict to zero there, as in §4.2. The summand `Sym^qV` has `H⁰ = 0` for `q > 0`, and `W_kH¹(M_{1,1}, Sym^{k−1}V) = S_{k+1}` by Eichler–Shimura (§0.5); straightening subsets containing a fixed marking identifies the hook Specht module.

*Needs:* §0.5; §3.1; SchurWeyl, Layers 3 and 5 (hook Specht modules and their dimensions); StableReductionPartII §§2.248 and 7.11.

**Checks.**

- At `n = k = 11` the group is `S₁₂`, of rational dimension two; at `n = 12, k = 11` it is `S₁₂^{11}` of dimension twenty-two, the hook `(2, 1^{10})` having dimension eleven.
- For odd `k + 1` there are no cusp forms of level one, so `W_k H^k(M_{1,n}) = 0` for every even `k ≥ 2`; at `k = 13`, `S₁₄ = 0` for every `n`.
- The multiplicity `binom(n−1, k−1)` is the dimension of the hook `(n−k+1, 1^{k−1})`, not `binom(n, k)`: subsets not containing the fixed marking are linear combinations of those that do.
- The insertion summands must be discarded before Eichler–Shimura is applied. The lowest-weight part of `H¹(M_{1,1}, V^{⊗(k−1)})` is `⊕_j (L^{(k−1−j)/2} S_{j+2})^{⊕μ(k−1,j)}` with `μ` as in §3.6; for `k = 13` it contains `(L S₁₂)^{⊕11}`, from the `μ(12, 10) = 12 − 1 = 11` copies of `Sym^{10}V ⊗ L` in `V^{⊗12}`, while `S₁₄ = 0`. These classes are insertions of `θ_{st}` and vanish on `M_{1,13}`, in agreement with `W₁₃H¹³(M_{1,13}) = 0`; a route that kept them would assign `(L S₁₂)^{⊕11}` to the summand with all twelve `i_s = 1`.

### 3.3 Even genus-one cohomology is tautological

For every `n ≥ 1` prove that `H^{2r}(M̄_{1,n})` is spanned by cycle classes of boundary strata, hence equals `RH^{2r}(M̄_{1,n})`, for every `r` (Petersen, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2; CLP, §2.2, pp. 5–7, and proof of Proposition 2.4, p. 7). Route: Petersen's vanishing of pure even cohomology of `M_{1,n}` in positive degree and the boundary exact sequence of §0.4, by induction on `n`. In particular every psi class on `M̄_{1,n}` is a rational combination of boundary classes.

*Needs:* §0.1; §0.4; §2.6; §3.2.

**Checks.**

- At `n = 1`, `ψ₁ = δ_irr/12` on `M̄_{1,1}`, with `δ_irr` the class of the boundary point in the stack normalization (degree `1/2`), so `deg ψ₁ = 1/24`.
- The statement is about cycle classes, not merely about the Tate type: an even class of type `(r, r)` that is not a boundary combination would violate it, and the even Tate type alone does not imply it.
- Odd cohomology is not covered: `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11}) ≠ 0`.

### 3.4 Homological stability and the stable cohomology

Prove: for every stable pair `(g, n)` with `g ≥ 2`, the natural map `ℚ[κ₁, κ₂, …] ⊗ ℚ[ψ₁, …, ψ_n] → H^*(M_{g,n}, ℚ)` is an isomorphism in degrees `k ≤ (2g − 2)/3` (Wahl, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2, for the stability ranges in Harer's theorem on surfaces with boundary and on closing the last boundary component; Madsen–Weiss, Theorem 1.1.1, for the stable cohomology `ℚ[κ₁, κ₂, …]`; Looijenga, Proposition (2.1), for the marked points, which are reached from the boundary case through its Gysin sequence). Route: by the comparison of §2.1, `H^*(M_{g,n}, ℚ) = H^*(Mod_{g,n}, ℚ)`; Harer stability with Wahl's ranges (isomorphism for `k ≤ (2g − 2)/3` under the genus-raising map and for `k ≤ 2g/3` under adding a boundary component or closing the last one, with Looijenga's Gysin sequence passing from a boundary component to a puncture; the minimum is `k ≤ (2g − 2)/3`) identifies the cohomology with its stable limit, the Madsen–Weiss theorem computes the unmarked stable cohomology, and the Gysin sequences of the marked points add the polynomial generators `ψᵢ`. The kappa classes here are the Mumford–Morita–Miller classes, which agree with §0.2 up to the normalization of `κ_a` as `π_*(ψ^{a+1})`.

*Needs:* MappingClassGroupsAndCanonicalRepresentations §2.3; §0.2; §2.1.

**Checks.**

- In degree two with `g ≥ 4`, the stable cohomology is spanned by `κ₁, ψ₁, …, ψ_n`, of dimension `n + 1`: this is the stable part of the Arbarello–Cornalba description of `H²` (AC, Theorem (2.2)).
- The range is a condition on `g`: `k ≤ (2g − 2)/3` is equivalent to `g ≥ 3k/2 + 1`. For `k = 2` this needs `g ≥ 4`; no claim is made for `M_3` in degree two.
- In degree one the stable cohomology vanishes, matching `H¹(M_{g,n}, ℚ) = 0` for `g ≥ 1`.

### 3.5 The stable range is tautological

For `g ≥ 2` and every `n`, prove that `H^k(M_{g,n})` is spanned by restrictions of tautological classes for `k ≤ (2g − 2)/3`, and that `H^k(M_{g,n}) = 0` for `k > 4g − 4 + n` (CLP, proof of Theorem 1.3, p. 10). These deliberately non-sharp bounds are those used in §4.9.

*Needs:* §3.4; §2.1; §0.1.

**Checks.**

- `H⁶(M_{g,n})` is tautological for `g ≥ 10`, since `6 ≤ (2·10 − 2)/3 = 6`; for `g = 9` the bound gives only degrees at most `5`.
- The vanishing bound `4g − 4 + n` is weaker than §2.1 for `n = 0` by one; either suffices for §4.9.

### 3.6 Cusp multiplicities of genus-one and genus-two moduli beyond the polynomial range

Let `V = R¹f_*ℚ` on `M_{1,1}` (§3.1 with `g = 1`), write `(x)_r = x(x − 1)⋯(x − r + 1)` for the falling factorial, and work in the Grothendieck groups of §1.2, in both realizations. Prove the following.

- Genus-one local systems. `e_c(M_{1,1}) = L`; for `k ≥ 1`, `H¹_c(M_{1,1}, Sym^{2k}V)` is an extension of the cusp realization `S_{2k+2}` of §0.5 by the weight-zero line of invariants of the unipotent local monodromy at the cusp and the other degrees vanish, so `e_c(M_{1,1}, Sym^{2k}V) = −S_{2k+2} − 1`; and `e_c(M_{1,1}, Sym^{2k+1}V) = 0`, the elliptic involution acting by `−1` (Getzler, semi-classical, §3, arXiv p. 7, where `S_{2k+2}` is the top weight graded piece of `H¹_c(M_{1,1}, Sym^{2k}V)`).
- Genus-one moduli. For `m ≥ 1`, `e_c(M_{1,m})` is obtained by expanding `(L − V)_{m−1}` in the representation ring of `SL₂` over `ℤ[L]`, with `V^{⊗r} ≅ ⊕_j (Sym^j V ⊗ L^{(r−j)/2})^{⊕μ(r,j)}`, `μ(r, j) = binom(r, (r−j)/2) − binom(r, (r−j)/2 − 1)` for `0 ≤ j ≤ r`, `j ≡ r (mod 2)` (each symplectic contraction `V ⊗ V → ℚ(−1)` carrying one `L`), and replacing each `L^i Sym^j V` by `L^i e_c(M_{1,1}, Sym^j V)`; and the `S_m`-equivariant class `e_c^{S_m}(M_{1,m})` is Getzler's residue formula (Getzler, semi-classical, §3, arXiv p. 8, recalling his computation for configuration spaces).
- Genus-one multiplicities. For `n ≥ 1`, `k ≥ 5` and `j ≥ 0`, the `S_{2k+2}`-isotypic part of `H^{2k+1+2j}(M̄_{1,n})^ss` is `(L^j S_{2k+2})^{⊕N}`, with `N` the coefficient of `L^j` in `Σ_{m=2k+1}^{n} c^{(2k)}_m(L) B_{n,m}(L)`, where `c^{(2k)}_m` is the multiplicity of `Sym^{2k}V` in the expansion of `(L − V)_{m−1}` and `B_{n,m}` is the sum, over the partitions of the `n` markings into `m` blocks, of the product of `e(M̄_{0,|B|+1})` over the blocks `B` with at least two elements; `e(M̄_{1,n})` is an integral combination of the classes `L^i` and `L^i S_{2k+2}`. In particular `H¹³(M̄_{1,13})^ss ≅ (L S₁₂)^{⊕429}`, `H¹³(M̄_{1,14})^ss ≅ (L S₁₂)^{⊕6006}` and `H¹⁵(M̄_{1,15})^ss ≅ S₁₆ ⊕ (L² S₁₂)^{⊕186263}` (Getzler, semi-classical, final table, p. 491, arXiv p. 10; CLP, proofs of Lemmas 7.5 and 7.6, p. 26, and Corollary 7.11, p. 28).
- Genus two. The Torelli morphism identifies the compact-type moduli stack `M₂^{ct} = M₂ ⊔ Sym² M_{1,1}`, where `Sym² M_{1,1} = [(M_{1,1} × M_{1,1})/S₂]` is the locus of two elliptic curves joined at their origins, with the moduli stack `A₂` of principally polarized abelian surfaces, carrying `V` to the standard local system (Petersen, genus two, §2.1, p. 4). For `a + b` odd, `e_c(M₂, V_{a,b}) = 0` (§3.1); for `a ≥ b ≥ 0` with `a + b` even and `a + b ≤ 12`, `e_c(M₂, V_{a,b}) = e_c(A₂, V_{a,b}) − e_c(Sym² M_{1,1}, V_{a,b})`, where `e_c(A₂, ℚ) = L³ + L²`, Poincaré dual to `H⁰(A₂) = ℚ` and `H²(A₂) = ℚ(−1)` (Petersen, genus two, Proposition 2.3, p. 6), for `(a, b) ≠ (0, 0)` `e_c(A₂, V_{a,b})` is the alternating sum of the semisimplified groups `H^k_c(A₂, V_{a,b})`, `k = 2, 3, 4`, of Petersen, abelian surfaces, Theorem 2.1, p. 4 (Remark 2.2, p. 4, for the Hodge realization), and `e_c(Sym² M_{1,1}, V_{a,b})` is the `S₂`-invariant part of the class on `M_{1,1} × M_{1,1}` given by the first item through the restriction of `V_{a,b}` to `(SL₂ × SL₂) ⋊ S₂`. Consequently `e_c^{S_n}(M_{2,n})`, `n ≤ 12`, is determined by these classes through the configuration spaces of `n` points on the fibres of the universal curve, of class `1 − V + L`, by relative Künneth and the decomposition of tensor powers of §3.1; and `H¹³(M̄_{2,12})^ss ≅ (L S₁₂)^{⊕264}` and `H¹⁵(M̄_{2,12})^ss ≅ (L² S₁₂)^{⊕836}` (CLP, equation (7.1), p. 24, and proof of Lemma 7.7, p. 26, which take both values from the Getzler–Kapranov formula in genus two).

Route: `M_{1,m}` is the complement of the zero section and the diagonals in the `(m − 1)`-fold fibre power of the universal elliptic curve over `M_{1,1}`, whose punctured fibre `E − 0` has class `L − V`; removing the diagonals one point at a time multiplies by `(L − V) − i`, and relative Künneth turns fibre products into tensor products of local systems. The genus-one local systems are Eichler–Shimura (§0.5) together with the local monodromy at the cusp. For the multiplicities apply §1.4 to the genus-zero, genus-one and genus-two classes; `H^q(M̄_{g,n})` is pure of weight `q`, so the weight-`(2k + 1 + 2j)` part of `e(M̄_{g,n})` is `−[H^{2k+1+2j}(M̄_{g,n})^ss]`. In genus one a stable graph contributes to the `S_{2k+2}`-isotypic part only through a genus-one vertex with at least `2k + 1` flags; such a graph is that vertex with rational trees attached, its legs are labelled and its automorphism group is trivial, and the trees carrying a block `B` of markings sum to `e(M̄_{0,|B|+1})`, which reduces §1.4 to the displayed sum. In genus two the automorphisms of loop and two-vertex graphs require the `S_m`-equivariant genus-one classes.

*Needs:* §§0.5, 1.2, 1.4, 1.5, 1.6 (genus-zero classes), 3.1; StableReductionPartII §§2.248 and 3.1–3.2 (universal curves and the separating clutching); PELModuli §M5.2 (the stack `A₂`); DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 (compact supports, weights and smooth proper purity, ℓ-adic); MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (the Hodge analogue); WeilConjectures, WC.1 (Künneth and localization for compact supports); SchurWeyl, Layers 7 and 9 (tensor powers of the symplectic representation).

**Checks.**

- `n = 12`, `S₁₂` (`k = 5`, `c_m = c^{(10)}_m`): `c₁₁ = 1` and `c₁₂ = Σ_{i=0}^{10} (L − i) = 11L − 55`, the `V^{⊗10}` term of `(L − V)_{11}` (`V^{⊗11}` has no `Sym^{10}V`); `B_{12,11} = binom(12, 2) = 66` and `B_{12,12} = 1`. The sum is `66 + 11L − 55 = 11 + 11L`: eleven copies of `S₁₂` in `H¹¹(M̄_{1,12})`, as in §4.7, and eleven of `L S₁₂` in `H¹³`, as §11.3 obtains by duality. Dropping the strata with a rational tail would leave `11L − 55`, a negative multiplicity.
- `n = 13`: `B_{13,11} = 286(L + 1) + 2145`, from one three-element block (`e(M̄_{0,4}) = L + 1`) or two two-element blocks (`78 · 55/2 = 2145`); `B_{13,12} = 78`; and `c₁₃ = 11L + σ₂(L, L − 1, …, L − 11) = 66L² − 715L + 1925`, the term `11L` being the eleven copies of `Sym^{10}V ⊗ L` in `V^{⊗12}`. The sum is `(286L + 2431) + (858L − 4290) + (66L² − 715L + 1925) = 66 + 429L + 66L²`, symmetric about `d = 13` as Poincaré duality requires, with degree-eleven coefficient `binom(12, 10) = 66` (§4.7). Omitting the twist of the contraction (`11` in place of `11L`) gives `77 + 418L + 66L²`, which is not symmetric.
- `n = 14` and `n = 15`: the sums are `286 + 6006L + 6006L² + 286L³` and `1001 + 53053L + 186263L² + 53053L³ + 1001L⁴`, with degree-eleven coefficients `binom(13, 10) = 286` and `binom(14, 10) = 1001` (§4.7); for `S₁₆` the sum at `n = 15` is `1`, from the `V^{⊗14}` term with `m = 15` alone. The printed `n = 14` row of Getzler's table is not symmetric about `d = 14` (Corrections to the sources).
- Genus two at `(a, b) = (0, 0)`: `e_c(Sym² M_{1,1}) = (L² + ψ₂(L))/2 = L²`, so `e_c(M₂) = L³ + L² − L² = L³`, matching `#H₂(𝔽_q) = q³` of §8.3, every genus-two curve being hyperelliptic.
- The quantities `s′_r` of Petersen's theorem, which count cusp forms with vanishing central `L`-value, do not enter: for `a = b` even the same term `s′_{a+b+4} L^{b+1}` occurs in `H²_c` and in `H³_c` and cancels; at `(4, 4)` it is `s′₁₂ L⁵`. In this range `S₁₂` enters `e_c(A₂, V_{a,b})` through the term `S_{a+3}` of `H³_c` at `(9, 1)` and `(9, 3)` and through `s₁₆ S₁₂(−2) = L² S₁₂` at `(11, 1)`; at `(10, 0)` the analogous term `s₁₄ S₁₂(−1)` vanishes.
- The `S₁₂`-isotypic part of `e(M̄_{2,12})` is `−(264L + 836L² + 264L³) S₁₂`: it has no term in `L⁰`, since `H¹¹(M̄_{2,12}) = 0` (§10.8), and it is symmetric about the middle weight `15 = d`; `264 = 66 + 132 + 66` and `836 = 825 + 11` are the counts of the graph families of §§11.6 and 11.9.

### Examples

`W₁₁H¹¹(M_{1,12}) ≅ S₁₂ ⊗ T_{(2,1^{10})}`, of dimension `2 · 11 = 22`, spanned by pullbacks along the eleven forgetful maps that forget one of the markings `2, …, 12`. On `M₂` every local system of odd weight has vanishing cohomology because the hyperelliptic involution acts by `−1`.

### Dependencies

Layers 0–2; StableReductionPartII §§2.248, 3.1–3.2, 7.11; PELModuli §M5.2; MappingClassGroupsAndCanonicalRepresentations §2.3; SchurWeyl, Layers 2, 3, 5, 7 and 9; DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory with gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge; WeilConjectures, WC.1.

## Layer 4: Semi-tautological extensions and finite generation

STEs are the algebras in which uniform generation statements make sense. The filling criterion of §4.5 strengthens the printed hypotheses of CLP Lemma 2.1, so that forgetting a marking in the same degree is justified.

### 4.1 The marking subspaces Φ and Ψ

For a stable pair `(g, n)` and `k ≥ 0` define `Φ^k_{g,n} ⊂ W_k H^k(M_{g,n})` as the sum of the images of the pullbacks `π_i^* : W_k H^k(M_{g,n−1}) → W_k H^k(M_{g,n})` along the forgetful maps `π_i` whose target is stable, and `Ψ^k_{g,n} = Σ_i ψ_i · Φ^{k−2}_{g,n}`. A sum over an empty set of stable forgetful targets is zero, and negative degrees contribute zero (CLP, §2.1, p. 4, and §3, p. 9).

*Needs:* §0.3; StableReductionPartII §§3.3 and 7.11 (forgetful maps and psi classes); §0.4 (the weight filtration of `M_{g,n}`).

API:

- Each pullback along a forgetful map to a stable target lands in `Φ^k_{g,n}`.
- `Ψ^k_{g,n}` is spanned by products of degree-two psi classes with classes in `Φ^{k−2}_{g,n}`.
- Relabelling the markings carries the summands of `Φ` and `Ψ` to the permuted summands; both are `S_n`-stable.
- There is no forgetful map from `M_{1,1}`, so `Φ^k_{1,1} = 0`.
- A pure degree-`k` class lies in `Φ^k_{g,n}` exactly when it is a sum of pullbacks from the stable targets obtained by forgetting one marking.
- A class lies in `Ψ^k_{g,n}` exactly when it is a sum of products `ψ_i x_i` with `x_i ∈ Φ^{k−2}_{g,n}`.

**Checks.**

- `Φ^k_{1,1} = 0` for every `k`, including `k = 0`, under the stable-target convention.
- `Φ⁰_{g,n} = ℚ = W₀H⁰(M_{g,n})` when `n > 0` and `(g, n − 1)` is stable.
- `Ψ¹_{g,n} = 0`, since `Φ^{−1} = 0`.

### 4.2 The local-system quotient of the marking filtration

For `g ≥ 2` prove `W_k H^k(M_{g,n}) / (Φ^k_{g,n} + Ψ^k_{g,n}) ≅ ⊕_{|λ|=n} W_k H^{k−n}(M_g, V_λ) ⊗ T_{λ^T}`, the sum over partitions of `n` with at most `g` parts, and that `W_k H^k(M_{g,n}) = Φ^k_{g,n}` when `n > 0` and `n ≥ k` (CLP, Lemma 3.1, pp. 9–10, using Petersen–Tavakol–Yin, §§5.1 and 5.2.2). Route: `M_{g,n}` is open in the `n`-fold fibre power `C^n` of the universal curve; the Leray decomposition and Künneth write `H^k(C^n)` as a sum over `(i_1, …, i_n)`; summands with some `i_s = 0` give `Φ`, those with some `i_s = 2` give `Ψ`, diagonal classes die on `M_{g,n}`, and the remaining summand `H^{k−n}(M_g, V^{⊗n})` modulo symplectic insertions is the right side by §3.1, with the signed action of §3.1, since `S_n` permutes the factors `H¹` of odd degree with Koszul signs. For `n ≥ k` a summand with `i_s = 2` forces another `i_{s′} = 0`, and `H⁰(M_g, V_λ) = 0` for nontrivial `λ`.

*Needs:* §§3.1, 4.1; SchurWeyl, Layers 3 and 9 (Specht modules, harmonic tensors, and the label conventions of SchurWeyl §9.7); StableReductionPartII §2.248; DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 and MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (Leray degeneration and purity for the smooth proper family `C^n → M_g`).

**Checks.**

- The Specht index is transposed: for `n = 2` the local system `V_{(2)} = Sym²V` pairs with the sign representation `T_{(1,1)}`, and `V_{(1,1)}` with the trivial one.
- For `n = 1` the quotient is `W_k H^{k−1}(M_g, V)`.
- For `n = k = 0` the equality `W₀H⁰ = Φ⁰` is not asserted: the hypothesis `n > 0` excludes it, and `Φ⁰_{g,0} = 0 ≠ ℚ`.

### 4.3 Even cohomology of genus-two moduli

For `0 ≤ r ≤ 10` and every `n ≥ 0` prove `W_{2r} H^{2r}(M_{2,n}) = RH^{2r}(M_{2,n})` and consequently `H^{2r}(M̄_{2,n}) = RH^{2r}(M̄_{2,n})` (CLP, Proposition 2.6, p. 7, and Corollary 2.7, p. 8; Petersen, genus two, Theorem 3.8 for `n < 20`). Route: for `n < 20` every even cohomology group of `M̄_{2,n}` is tautological by Petersen's theorem, proved through the compact-type moduli `M^{ct}_{2,n}` and the local systems on `M^{ct}_2 ≅ A₂`, and restriction gives the open statement; for `n ≥ 20 ≥ 2r` the second part of §4.2 gives `W_{2r}H^{2r}(M_{2,n}) = Φ^{2r}_{2,n}`, which is tautological by induction on `n` because forgetful pullbacks preserve tautological classes. Lift to `M̄_{2,n}`, for `n ≥ 20`, by §0.4 and induction on `n`: the kernel of `H^{2r}(M̄_{2,n}) → W_{2r}H^{2r}(M_{2,n})` is the image of `H^{2r−2}(D̃)(−1)`, whose Künneth factors live on `M̄_{1,n+2}`, on products `M̄_{1,a} × M̄_{1,b}` and on products `M̄_{0,a} × M̄_{2,b}` with `b ≤ n − 1`; genus zero has no odd cohomology, and two factors of odd degree in genus one would both have degree at least eleven (§2.9), which `2r − 2 ≤ 18` excludes; so every factor has even degree at most eighteen and is tautological, by §2.6 in genus zero, §3.3 in genus one and the induction in genus two. Clutching pushforwards of tautological classes are tautological (§0.1), and a class of `W_{2r}H^{2r}(M_{2,n}) = RH^{2r}(M_{2,n})` lifts to `RH^{2r}(M̄_{2,n})` by definition. The filling criterion §4.5 is not the route: with `S = RH^*` its hypothesis fails at the odd triple `(1, 11, 11)`, which it calls on whenever `n ≥ 9`, `r ≥ 6` and `2n − 2r ≥ 5`.

*Needs:* §§4.2, 0.1, 0.4, 2.6, 2.9, 3.1, 3.3; §3.6 (the Torelli identification `M₂^{ct} ≅ A₂`, on which Petersen's theorem rests); PELModuli §M5.2 (the stack `A₂`).

**Checks.**

- The bound is cohomological degree `20`, that is `r ≤ 10`, not Chow codimension twenty.
- `H²²(M̄_{2,20})` contains a non-tautological algebraic class, so the range cannot be extended to `r = 11` (Graber–Pandharipande, Theorem 2, p. 3, recalled in CLP, Introduction, p. 3). The class is that of the closure `Z` of the locus of bielliptic genus-two curves with ten pairs of markings exchanged by the bielliptic involution: bielliptic genus-two curves form a two-dimensional family and the ten pairs add ten dimensions, so `dim Z = 12` and `Z` has codimension `23 − 12 = 11` in `M̄_{2,20}`, of dimension `d = 23`; it meets the boundary divisor `M̄_{1,11} × M̄_{1,11}`, of dimension `22`, in the diagonal, of codimension `11` there (Graber–Pandharipande, §1, p. 3). The pushforward of that diagonal to `M̄_{2,20}` is a different class, of codimension `12` and degree `24`.
- §4.5 with `S = RH^*` is not the route. At `(2, 20)` with `r = 6`, `(1, 11) ≺ (2, 20)`, `2d_{1,11} − 11 = 11 ≤ 2d_{2,20} − 12 = 34` and `11 ≤ 12`, so §4.5 calls on `(1, 11, 11)`, where the quotient is `S₁₂` and `RH¹¹ = 0`. The parity argument needs no odd triple: `H^{10}` of a divisor `M̄_{1,a} × M̄_{1,b}` has no summand `H^i ⊗ H^j` with `i` and `j` odd, since both would be at least eleven.

### 4.4 Semi-tautological extensions

An STE is a collection of unital graded `ℚ`-subalgebras `S^*(M̄_{g,n}) ⊂ H^*(M̄_{g,n})`, one for each stable pair, containing `RH^*(M̄_{g,n})` and closed under pullback along forgetful morphisms and relabellings and under pushforward along clutching morphisms, including clutching of exterior products of classes on the factors (CLP, Definition 1.2, p. 2). Closure under forgetful pushforward or clutching pullback is not part of the definition. The STE generated by a family of subspaces is the intersection of all STEs containing it; an STE is motivic if each `S^*` is a sub-Hodge structure and its `ℚ_ℓ`-span is `G_ℚ`-stable.

*Needs:* §0.1; StableReductionPartII §§3.1–3.2 and 7.11.

API:

- The STE generated by a family is contained in an STE `S` if and only if `S` contains the family.
- Enlarging the generating family enlarges the generated STE.
- The empty family generates `RH^*`.
- Every class of the STE generated by a family is obtained from members of the family and from tautological classes by finitely many cup products, forgetful pullbacks, relabellings and clutching pushforwards of exterior products. When the members are sub-Hodge structures whose `ℚ_ℓ`-spans are `G_ℚ`-stable, the degree-`k` part at each stable pair is therefore a sum of images, under morphisms of Hodge structures and of Galois representations, of spaces `L^j ⊗ G₁ ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ G_r`, with `G_i` members of positive degrees `k_i` and `k₁ + ⋯ + k_r + 2j = k`: products add degrees, forgetful pullbacks preserve them, a clutching pushforward adds two and one twist `L`, and tautological classes span copies of powers of `L`. A normal form by decorated stable graphs whose vertex decorations are products of forgetful pullbacks of members and of tautological classes is not asserted in general, since the cup product of two graph pushforwards brings in clutching pullbacks of members, under which an STE need not be closed; for `S_ω` it holds (§4.8).
- Two STEs with the same subalgebras at every stable pair are equal.
- An STE contains the unit and `RH^*` and is closed under relabelling, forgetful pullback and clutching pushforward of exterior products.

**Checks.**

- The full cohomology system `H^*` is an STE, and so is the system of subrings generated by algebraic cycle classes.
- The empty family generates `RH^*`, not the zero system: the unit is required.
- `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` is not in the STE generated by the empty family, since every class there has even degree.
- Products of members on one vertex occur. For disjoint eleven-element sets `A`, `B` of the twenty-two markings of `M̄_{1,22}`, the class `f_A^*ω · f_B^*ω ∈ H²²(M̄_{1,22})` lies in `S_ω` and is the image of `S₁₂ ⊗ S₁₂`, the case `r = 2`, `j = 0`, `k = 22` of the description above; it is not a single forgetful pullback of a member multiplied by a tautological class.

### 4.5 The filling criterion

Let `S` be an STE, `(g, n)` a stable pair and `k ≥ 0`. Suppose that `S^{k′}(M̄_{g′,n′}) → W_{k′} H^{k′}(M_{g′,n′}) / (Φ^{k′}_{g′,n′} + RH^{k′}(M_{g′,n′}))` is surjective for `(g′, n′, k′) = (g, n, k)` and for every `(g′, n′, k′)` with `(g′, n′) ≺ (g, n)`, `2d_{g′,n′} − k′ ≤ 2d_{g,n} − k` and `0 ≤ k′ ≤ k`. Then `S^k(M̄_{g,n}) = H^k(M̄_{g,n})`. This corrects CLP, Lemma 2.1, pp. 4–5, whose hypothesis (2.2) requires only `k′ ≤ k − 2`; its proof also uses `S^k(M̄_{g,n−1}) = H^k(M̄_{g,n−1})`, a same-degree case that (2.2) does not provide. Route: induct on the stable-pair order; the enlarged hypothesis passes to every boundary Künneth factor (degree at most `k − 2`) and to every stable forgetful source in degree `k`; lift `Φ` from those sources, `RH` by definition, and the remaining quotient by assumption; §0.4 gives the equality.

*Needs:* §§4.1, 4.4; §0.3; §0.4.

**Checks.**

- The printed hypothesis is insufficient. Take `S = RH^*` and `(g, n, k) = (1, 12, 11)`. By §3.2 every class of `W₁₁H¹¹(M_{1,12})` is a pullback along a forgetful map, so the quotient at `(1, 12, 11)` vanishes; all triples with `k′ ≤ 9` in genus at most one have vanishing quotient, since `W_{k′}H^{k′}(M_{1,n′}) = 0` for `1 ≤ k′ ≤ 9`, and genus zero is tautological. Yet `RH¹¹ = 0` while `dim H¹¹(M̄_{1,12}) = 22`. The corrected hypothesis includes `(1, 11, 11)`, where the quotient is `S₁₂ ≠ 0`, and so excludes this case.
- The hypothesis at `(g, n, k)` itself cannot be dropped: for `S = RH^*` at `(1, 11, 11)` every smaller triple is fine but the conclusion fails.
- For `k = 0` the criterion reduces to the unit: `W₀H⁰(M_{g,n}) = ℚ = RH⁰`.

### 4.6 The odd-marking reduction in genus two

For odd `n ≥ 1` and every `k ≥ 2` let the two-cusp boundary space `T^k_n ⊂ H^k(M̄_{2,n})` be the span of the clutching pushforwards `ξ_*(α ⊗ β)` along the separating clutchings `M̄_{1,A∪{p}} × M̄_{1,B∪{q}} → M̄_{2,n}`, `A ⊔ B = {1, …, n}`, of exterior products of odd-degree classes `α`, `β` with `deg α + deg β = k − 2`. Prove that the STE generated by `H^k(M̄_{2,n−1})`, `H^{k−2}(M̄_{2,n−1})`, `H^{k−2}(M̄_{1,m})` for `1 ≤ m ≤ n + 2`, and `T^k_n`, contains `H^k(M̄_{2,n})`; and that `T^k_n = 0` when `k` is odd or `k ≤ 23`, so that in these degrees the STE generated by the first three families alone contains `H^k(M̄_{2,n})`, which is CLP, Lemma 3.2, p. 10, in these degrees. For even `k ≥ 24` the printed lemma omits `T^k_n` from the generators; its proof does not show that `T^k_n` lies in the STE generated by the three families, and this roadmap neither asserts nor refutes that containment (Scope and ownership; Corrections to the sources). Route: by §4.2 the quotient by `Φ + Ψ` is pure cohomology of local systems of odd weight `n` on `M₂`, killed by the hyperelliptic involution (§3.1); `Φ` and `Ψ` come from `(2, n − 1)`; by §0.4 the rest of `H^k(M̄_{2,n})` is spanned by the Gysin images `ξ_*(γ)` of classes `γ ∈ H^{k−2}` of the normalized one-edge strata, and these are not generators as they stand. A loop stratum `(1, n + 2)` carries a generator. On a stratum `(0, n₁) × (2, n₂)`, `n₂ ≤ n − 1`, write the genus-zero factor as a combination of undecorated boundary strata classes (§2.6, with the excess intersection formula of §0.1 to rewrite products of boundary divisors), so the class is a sum of `ξ_{Γ*}(β ⊗ 1 ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ 1)` over trees `Γ` with one genus-two vertex and genus-zero vertices; re-associate the clutching so that the last clutching is along the edge `e` to a genus-zero leaf `w`: the class glued is a degree-`(k − 2)` class `γ` on the contraction `(2, m)` of `Γ − w`, with `m ≤ n − 1`, and `ξ_{e*}(γ ⊗ 1) = [D_e] · π^*γ` by the projection formula for the forgetful map `π` that contracts `w`, where `π^*γ` factors through `H^{k−2}(M̄_{2,n−1})`, a generator, since the STE is closed under forgetful pullback. On a separating stratum `(1, n₁) × (1, n₂)`, `n₁ + n₂ = n + 2`, write `γ` by Künneth as a sum of classes `α ⊗ β`. If both factors are odd the term lies in `T^k_n`; then `k` is even, and since odd genus-one cohomology vanishes through degree nine (§2.9), both degrees are at least eleven and `k ≥ 24`, which gives `T^k_n = 0` for odd `k` and for `k ≤ 23`. If both are even the term is tautological, an even genus-one class being a combination of boundary strata classes (§3.3). Otherwise expanding the even factor writes the term as a sum of `ξ_{Γ*}(α ⊗ 1 ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ 1)` over genus-two graphs `Γ` with one distinguished vertex. If `Γ` has a cycle, cut one of its edges, so that the last clutching is the loop clutching of a degree-`(k − 2)` class on `(1, n + 2)`, a generator; otherwise `Γ` is a tree with a leaf `w` other than the distinguished vertex, and `Γ − w` contracts to `(1, m)` with `m ≤ n + 1` if `w` has genus one, a generator, or to `(2, m)` with `m ≤ n − 1` if `w` has genus zero, the projection-formula case above.

*Needs:* §§3.1, 4.2, 4.4; §0.4; §§0.1, 2.6, 2.9, 3.3 (strata expansions of tautological classes in genus at most one and the vanishing of odd genus-one classes through degree nine); StableReductionPartII §§3.1–3.2, 3.10 and 7.11 (forgetful and clutching morphisms and their composites on boundary strata); §1.6 with WeilConjectures, WC.5 and WC.6 (no odd cohomology on genus-one moduli with at most ten markings, for the vanishing of `T^k_n` when `n ≤ 19`).

API:

- `T^k_n` is the image of the sum, over the unordered partitions `{A, B}` of the markings and the odd `a, b` with `a + b = k − 2`, of the Gysin maps `ξ_{A,B*} : (H^a(M̄_{1,A∪{p}}) ⊗ H^b(M̄_{1,B∪{q}}))(−1) → H^k(M̄_{2,n})` of the separating divisors; each raises the degree by two with one Tate twist, the divisor having codimension one, and is a morphism of Hodge structures and of Galois representations, so `T^k_n` is a sub-Hodge structure whose `ℚ_ℓ`-span is `G_ℚ`-stable.
- Relabelling by `σ ∈ S_n` carries the stratum of `{A, B}` to that of `{σ(A), σ(B)}`, so `T^k_n` is `S_n`-stable.
- Exchanging the two factors identifies the stratum of `(A, B)` with that of `(B, A)`, and `ξ_{A,B*}(α ⊗ β) = −ξ_{B,A*}(β ⊗ α)` for `α`, `β` of odd degree, the Koszul sign of exchanging two odd classes.
- `T^k_n = 0` when `k` is odd, since `a + b = k − 2` is then odd, and when `k ≤ 23`, since `a, b ≥ 11` by §2.9.
- `T^k_n = 0` when `n ≤ 19`: a genus-one factor with at most ten flags has polynomial point count (§1.6), hence no odd cohomology, so both factors need at least eleven flags, and they carry `n + 2` flags together.
- Every STE that contains `H^a(M̄_{1,m})` for all odd `a ≤ k − 13` and all `m ≤ n + 1` contains `T^k_n`, by closure under clutching pushforward of exterior products.

**Checks.**

- `n = 11, k = 13` and `n = 13, k = 15` give the reductions used in §11.10, with `T^{13}_{11} = T^{15}_{13} = 0`.
- With `n = 13` and `k = 15`, the stratum `(1, 12) × (1, 3)` carries `β ⊗ ψ_m` with `β ∈ H¹¹(M̄_{1,12})` and `ψ_m ∈ H²(M̄_{1,3})`; `β` is not in the generated STE, whose degree-eleven part is `RH¹¹ = 0` because every generator has degree thirteen or fifteen and the closure operations never lower degree. Expanding `ψ_m` into the boundary divisors of `M̄_{1,3}`, which are `δ_irr` and `δ_{0,S}` for the four subsets `S` of at least two of the three flags, the graphs that can occur are `(1,12)—(0,5)` with a loop at `(0,5)`, `(1,12)—(0,3)—(1,2)`, `(1,12)—(1,2)—(0,3)` and `(1,12)—(0,4)—(1,1)`; re-associating, the last clutching glues a degree-thirteen class on `(1, 15)`, `(1, 13)`, `(2, 12)` and `(1, 14)` respectively, each a generator, the third through the projection formula. The bounds `m ≤ n + 2 = 15`, `m ≤ n − 1 = 12` and `m ≤ n + 1 = 14` are attained by the first, third and fourth graphs.
- `T^{23}_n = 0` for every `n`, the degree being odd, and degree `24` is the first that allows a two-cusp class, with `(a, b) = (11, 11)` only. `T^{24}_1 = 0`: the two genus-one vertices carry `n + 2 = 3` flags together, while each needs eleven for `H¹¹ ≠ 0` (§4.7); for odd `n` the first possibly nonzero case is `T^{24}_{21}`, from `11 + 12 = 23 = n + 2` flags. The term `β ⊗ ψ_m` of the previous Check has an even factor and does not enter `T^{15}_{13} = 0`; a definition admitting one even factor would put its pushforward into `T^{15}_{13}`, among the generators, and so skip the re-association step that places it in the STE of the three families. These Checks do not assert that any allowed Gysin image is nonzero.
- At `(n, k) = (23, 24)`, one of the cases in which the printed form is not proved (for odd `n` the first is `(21, 24)`, whose strata with `|A| ∈ {10, 11}` carry `H¹¹ ⊗ H¹¹`), the question concerns cohomology classes, not formal tensors. Let `V` be the sum, over the separating strata whose sides have `{|A|, |B|} = {10, 13}` or `{11, 12}` markings, of `H¹¹(M̄_{1,A∪{p}}) ⊗ H¹¹(M̄_{1,B∪{q}})` and `G : V(−1) → H²⁴(M̄_{2,23})` the sum of the Gysin maps, with image `T^{24}_{23}`. Forgetful pullback from `(2, 22)` acts on symbols by `[A′, α ⊗ β] ↦ [A′ ∪ x, f_x^*α ⊗ β] + [A′, α ⊗ f_x^*β]`, and with `ε` the sum of coefficients in the bases `f_C^*`, `C ∋ p` (respectively `C ∋ q`), of `Hom(S₁₂, H¹¹)` of §4.7, the sign-weighted sum `(−1)^{|A|} ε(α) ε(β)` vanishes on these formal pullbacks and equals one on the symbol `[A, ω ⊗ ω_C]` with `|A| = 10`. This is a statement about `V`; it does not place `T^{24}_{23}` outside the generated STE. The functional is not shown to descend through `G`: the kernel of the Gysin map from all normalized one-edge strata, modulo the images of the two-edge strata, is `Gr^W_{24} H^{23}(M_{2,23})` by the weight spectral sequence, and nothing excludes relations among these symbols there. And the generated STE also contains the products `[D_{A,B}] · c = ξ_{A,B*}(ξ_{A,B}^* c)` for every `c ∈ H²²(M̄_{2,23})` (which lies in it by this target at `k = 22`, `H²⁰(M̄_{2,22})` and `H²⁰(M̄_{1,m})` being tautological by §§4.3 and 3.3), whose `H¹¹ ⊗ H¹¹` components the formal calculation does not account for.
- For even `n` the argument fails: local systems of even weight on `M₂` can have pure cohomology, for instance `W₁₇H¹⁷(M_{2,14}) ≠ 0` (CLP, Introduction, p. 3).

### 4.7 The eleventh cohomology group in genus one

For `n ≥ 1` prove that `H¹¹(M̄_{1,n}) = 0` for `n ≤ 10` and, for `n ≥ 11`, `H¹¹(M̄_{1,n}) ≅ S₁₂ ⊗ T_{(n−10, 1^{10})}`, of rank `2 binom(n−1, 10)`, spanned by the pullbacks `ω_A = f_A^*ω` along the forgetful maps `f_A : M̄_{1,n} → M̄_{1,A} ≅ M̄_{1,11}`, `A` an ordered eleven-element set of markings and `ω ∈ H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})`, the maps `f_A^*` for increasing `A ∋ 1` forming a basis of `Hom(S₁₂, H¹¹(M̄_{1,n}))`. For a set `B` of markings whose complement has `|B^c| ≥ 2`, so that the boundary divisor `ι_B : D_B = M̄_{1,B∪{p}} × M̄_{0,B^c∪{q}} → M̄_{1,n}` exists, prove `ι_B^*ω_A = pr₁^*ω_{ε(A)}` when `|A ∩ B| ≥ 10`, where `ε(A)` replaces the element of `A` outside `B`, if there is one, by `p`, and `ι_B^*ω_A = 0` otherwise; the other boundary divisors have no `H¹¹`. For `n ≥ 12` prove that pullback `H¹¹(M̄_{1,n}) → ⊕_{|B|=10} H¹¹(D_B)`, the sum over the ten-element sets `B` of markings, is injective; every such `D_B` exists, since `|B^c| = n − 10 ≥ 2`. For `n = 11` the injectivity fails: a ten-element `B` would leave the rational factor `M̄_{0,2}`, which is not stable, so the sum is empty, and every boundary divisor of `M̄_{1,11}` has zero `H¹¹`, so the restriction of the rank-two group `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` to the boundary is zero (CLP-H11, Lemma 2.1, p. 4, Lemma 2.2, p. 6, Proposition 2.3 and Corollaries 2.4–2.5, p. 7, where Corollary 2.5 is printed without the bound `n ≥ 12`; see the Corrections to the sources). Route: the normalized boundary strata of `M̄_{1,n}` are products of moduli spaces of genus at most one, whose odd cohomology vanishes through degree nine (§2.9), so §0.4 gives `H¹¹(M̄_{1,n}) ≅ W₁₁H¹¹(M_{1,n})`, described with its basis by §3.2; the boundary formula follows from the commuting squares of forgetful and clutching maps, a proper boundary divisor of `M̄_{1,11}` having no `H¹¹`; for `n ≥ 12` injectivity follows by matching the images of the `ω_A` with the Specht-module generators in the tabloid space of shape `(n − 10, 1^{10})`, each tabloid being the component of an existing divisor `D_B`; at `n = 11` the tabloids of shape `(1, 1^{10})` correspond to no divisor. The vanishing of `H¹¹` in genus at least two is §10.9.

*Needs:* §§0.4, 0.5, 2.9, 3.2; SchurWeyl, Layers 1 and 3 (tabloids and Specht modules); StableReductionPartII §§3.1 and 7.11 (separating clutching and forgetful maps).

**Checks.**

- `H¹¹(M̄_{1,10}) = 0`, consistent with the polynomial count of §1.6.
- `dim H¹¹(M̄_{1,12}) = 2·11 = 22`; `dim H¹¹(M̄_{1,13}) = 2·66 = 132`.
- The boundary rule depends on `|A ∩ B|`, not on whether `A` meets `B`: for `n = 12` and `B^c = {x, y}`, `ω_A` with `x, y ∈ A` restricts to zero on `D_B` (`|A ∩ B| = 9`), while `ω_A` with `x ∈ A`, `y ∉ A` restricts to `pr₁^*ω_{ε(A)} ≠ 0`.
- The bound `n ≥ 12` in the injectivity is needed: at `n = 11` the source `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` has rank two, there is no divisor `D_B` with `|B| = 10`, and the restriction to the boundary is `ℚ² → 0` (compare the first Check of §2.4). At `n = 12` the source has rank `22` and the divisors `D_B`, `|B| = 10`, have three-pointed rational factors, so the Specht-module injection applies.

### 4.8 Cusp-generated extensions are closed under all tautological operations

Let `S_ω` be the STE generated by `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})`. Prove that `S_ω` contains `H¹¹(M̄_{1,n})` and `H¹³(M̄_{1,n})` for every `n`, and that `S_ω` is closed under forgetful pushforward and clutching pullback; the same holds for the STE generated by any rational sub-Hodge structures of the genus-one cusp realizations `S_{k+1} ⊂ H^k(M̄_{1,k})`, with their Galois span in the ℓ-adic realization (CLP, Corollary 2.3, Proposition 2.4 and Remark 2.5, p. 7). That `S_ω` contains `H¹¹(M̄_{g,n})` for every stable pair follows with §10.9. Route: `H¹¹(M̄_{1,n})` is spanned by forgetful pullbacks of `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` (§4.7); `S₁₄ = 0` forces `H¹³(M̄_{1,n})` onto the boundary (§§0.4, 3.2), whose degree-eleven classes are exterior products of genus-one `H¹¹` classes with units; every class of `S_ω` is a decorated graph whose non-tautological decorations are `H¹¹`-classes on genus-one vertices (products of two odd classes in genus one are boundary combinations by §3.3); forgetful pushforward of `H¹¹(M̄_{1,n})` lands in `H⁹(M̄_{1,n−1}) = 0` (§2.9); the boundary formula of §4.7 and the excess intersection formula give closure under clutching pullback.

*Needs:* §§0.4, 2.9, 3.2, 3.3, 4.4, 4.7.

**Checks.**

- An arbitrary complex vector of `S₁₂ ⊗ ℂ` does not generate a motivic STE: the generated STE is that of its rational sub-Hodge structure, here all of `S₁₂`, since `S₁₂` is simple of rank two.
- The pushforward of a class of `H¹¹(M̄_{1,12})` along `M̄_{1,12} → M̄_{1,11}` lands in `H⁹(M̄_{1,11}) = 0`.

### 4.9 Finite generation in each cohomological degree

For every `k ≥ 0` prove that the STE generated by the groups `H^{k′}(M̄_{g′,n′})` with `k′ ≤ k`, `g′ < 3k′/2 + 1`, `n′ ≤ k′` and `4g′ − 4 + n′ ≥ k′` contains `H^k(M̄_{g,n})` for every stable pair; the set of such triples is finite (CLP, Theorem 1.3 and its proof, pp. 2 and 10). Route: apply §4.5; outside the region the quotient of §4.5 vanishes: for `g′ ≥ 2` by §4.2 when `n′ > k′` and by §3.5 when `k′ > 4g′ − 4 + n′` or `k′ ≤ (2g′ − 2)/3`; in genus one by §3.2, which makes `W_{k′}H^{k′}(M_{1,n′})` a sum of forgetful pullbacks when `n′ > k′ ≥ 1` and zero when `k′ > n′` (the condition `k′ > 4g′ − 4 + n′` in genus one), the remaining genus-one triples outside the region having `k′ = 0`, where `W₀H⁰ = ℚ = RH⁰`; and by §2.6 in genus zero. Inside the region the generators supply surjectivity.

*Needs:* §§2.6, 3.2, 3.5, 4.2, 4.5.

**Checks.**

- The genus bound is strict and the other two are weak: for `k′ = 4` the region allows `g′ ≤ 6`, not `g′ = 7`.
- There is no seed at `(k′, g′, n′) = (0, 0, 0)`, which is unstable; degree zero is the unit of `RH^*`.
- For `k = 11` the seed `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11})` lies in the region (`1 < 17.5`, `11 ≤ 11`, `4 − 4 + 11 ≥ 11`).
- The triple `(g′, n′, k′) = (1, 10, 11)` lies outside the region (`4 − 4 + 10 = 10 < 11`) and is of genus one, where §3.5 does not apply; its quotient vanishes because `W₁₁H¹¹(M_{1,10}) = 0` by §3.2, ten markings being fewer than eleven.

### 4.10 Finite generation for homology

For fixed `k ≥ 0` prove that there is a finitely generated STE `S` with `S^{2d−j}(M̄_{g,n}) = H^{2d−j}(M̄_{g,n})` for every stable pair and every `0 ≤ j ≤ k`; one generating family consists of the groups `H^{2d−j}(M̄_{g,n})`, `0 ≤ j ≤ min(k, 2d)`, at the pairs with `g ≥ 1` and either `n ≤ 1, 2g ≤ k` or `n ≥ 2, 2g − 2 + n ≤ k` (CLP, Remark 3.3, p. 10, which asserts the existence; the explicit region is derived here from §2.3). Route: induct on `j` and then on the stable-pair order; outside the region `H_j^{BM}(M_{g,n}) = 0` by §2.3, so by §0.4 `H_j(M̄_{g,n})` is pushed forward from the boundary, whose Künneth factors have homological degree at most `j`; genus zero is tautological.

*Needs:* §§0.4, 2.3, 2.6, 4.4.

**Checks.**

- Each group is translated with its own `d`: a fixed homological degree `j` is cohomological degree `2d_{g,n} − j`, which varies with the pair.
- For `k = 13` the region in genus one is `n ≤ 13`, in genus two `n ≤ 11`.

### 4.11 Finitely many simple realizations in each degree

For each `k` prove that only finitely many isomorphism classes of simple rational Hodge structures, respectively of simple `ℚ_ℓ[G_ℚ]`-modules, occur in `⊕_{g,n} H^k(M̄_{g,n})^ss` (CLP, Corollary 1.4, p. 2). Route: by §4.9 and the description of a generated STE in §4.4, `H^k(M̄_{g,n})` is a sum of images of morphisms from spaces `L^j ⊗ G₁ ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ G_r`, the `G_i` seed groups of §4.9 of positive degree with `Σ_i deg G_i + 2j = k`; there are finitely many seeds and `r ≤ k`, so the simple constituents of `H^k(M̄_{g,n})^ss` lie among those of finitely many such tensor products.

*Needs:* §§4.4, 4.9.

**Checks.**

- The list depends on `k`; no finiteness across all degrees is asserted, and `S₁₂ L^m` occurs in degree `11 + 2m` for every `m ≥ 0`.
- In degree eleven the list is `{S₁₂}` (§§4.7 and 10.9); in degree thirteen it is `{L S₁₂}` and in degree fourteen `{L⁷}` (§12.9). Neither list is empty: `H¹³(M̄_{1,12})^ss ≅ (L S₁₂)^{⊕11}` (§3.6), and the top-degree class of `M̄_{0,10}`, of dimension seven, spans a copy of `L⁷` (§2.6).

### 4.12 Top-heaviness

Let `S` be an STE, `(g, n)` stable, `d = d_{g,n}`, and `a` a tautological divisor class that is the pullback of an ample class on the coarse space. Prove that cup product with `a^{d−k}` is injective `S^k(M̄_{g,n}) → S^{2d−k}(M̄_{g,n})` for `0 ≤ k ≤ d`, and that cup product with `a` is injective `S^k → S^{k+2}` for `k < d`; hence `dim S^k ≤ dim S^{2d−k}` and the dimensions are nondecreasing up to the middle in each parity (CLP, Introduction, p. 3). Route: hard Lefschetz on `H^*` restricted to `S`, which `a` preserves because it is tautological; if `ax = 0` with `k < d` then `a^{d−k}x = 0`, so `x = 0`. CLP also asserts that dimensions decrease above the middle; that requires a separate argument and is not part of this target.

*Needs:* §4.4; StableReductionPartII, gap:pointed-coarse-projectivity; DeligneWeightsAndPurity, gap:dm-stack-hard-lefschetz; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge.

**Checks.**

- No perfect pairing on `S` is claimed: top-heaviness allows `dim S^k < dim S^{2d−k}`, and for `S = RH^*` with `k ≥ 22` the pairing can fail to be perfect (Petersen–Tommasi, recalled in CLP, p. 3).
- For `M̄_{0,4}`, `d = 1`: `a` maps `S⁰ = ℚ` isomorphically onto `S² = ℚ`.

### Examples

For `k = 13` the seeds of §4.9 in degree `k′ = 13` are the groups `H¹³(M̄_{g′,n′})` with `g′ ≤ 20`, `n′ ≤ 13` and `4g′ − 4 + n′ ≥ 13`; in genus one this is `n′ = 13` only, and in genus two `9 ≤ n′ ≤ 13`. The STE `S_ω` contains every genus-one `H¹¹` and `H¹³` (§4.8), every `H¹¹` by §10.9, and by Layer 12 every `H¹³`.

### Dependencies

Layers 0–3; StableReductionPartII §§2.248, 3.1–3.3, 3.10, 7.11, with gap:pointed-coarse-projectivity; PELModuli §M5.2; DeligneWeightsAndPurity §8.24 with gap:dm-stack-hard-lefschetz; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory with gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge; SchurWeyl, Layers 1, 3 and 9.

## Layer 5: Chow–Künneth generation and pure algebraicity

The Chow–Künneth generation property quantifies over every test stack. On an open stack it controls exactly the lowest-weight cohomology.

### 5.1 The Chow–Künneth generation property

A smooth finite-type algebraic stack `X` over a field `k`, stratified by quotient stacks, has the Chow–Künneth generation property (CKgP) if for every finite-type algebraic stack `Y` over `k` stratified by quotient stacks the exterior product `A_*(X) ⊗_ℚ A_*(Y) → A_*(X × Y)` is surjective (CLP, Definition 4.1, p. 11). `Y` need be neither smooth nor proper; the universal quantifier is part of the definition.

*Needs:* SchemeAndStackFoundations, gap:stack-rational-chow (Chow groups and exterior products of stacks stratified by quotient stacks).

API:

- For every admissible `Y`, each class on `X × Y` is a finite sum of exterior products.
- A class of dimension `r` on `X × Y` is a sum of exterior products of total dimension `r`, by projecting to the graded piece.
- An isomorphism of stacks preserves the CKgP.
- `Spec k` has the CKgP, its exterior product being the identity.

**Checks.**

- A point has the CKgP; the exterior product with any `Y` is the identity of `A_*(Y)`.
- `ℙ¹` has the CKgP, by localization from a point and `𝔸¹`, with `A_*(ℙ¹ × Y)` generated by the point and the fundamental class.
- A complex elliptic curve `E` does not have the CKgP: the diagonal of `E × E` has a component in `H¹ ⊗ H¹`, which no sum of exterior products of algebraic classes reaches. Testing only `Y = Spec k` would not detect this.

### 5.2 Permanence of the Chow–Künneth generation property

Within smooth finite-type stacks stratified by quotient stacks prove: the CKgP passes to open substacks; it descends along proper surjective morphisms representable by DM stacks; it holds for a stack with a finite stratification whose strata have it; it is equivalent for the total space and base of an affine bundle; it passes to Grassmann bundles; and `BGL_r`, `BSL_r`, `BPGL_r` have it (CLP, Proposition 4.2, p. 11, citing Canning–Larson §3.1). Route: localization after product with every `Y`; rational surjectivity of proper pushforward via a generically finite multisection; homotopy invariance; the Grassmann bundle formula; finite-dimensional approximations of classifying stacks.

*Needs:* §5.1; §0.6; SchemeAndStackFoundations, gap:stack-rational-chow; EquivariantIntersectionTheory §§2.2–2.3 (homotopy invariance for equivariant affine bundles and `A^*_{GL_r} = ℤ[c₁, …, c_r]`).

**Checks.**

- `BC₂` over a field of characteristic not two has the CKgP: `Spec k → BC₂` is proper, surjective and representable (a `C₂`-torsor), and `A^*(BC₂ × Y) = A^*(Y)` rationally. With integral coefficients `A¹(BC₂) = ℤ/2` would not be reached; the rational convention is essential.
- A Grassmann bundle of a vector bundle has the CKgP if its base does; a Brauer–Severi scheme without a vector-bundle presentation is not covered by this clause and is treated through a lift (§7.4).

### 5.3 The Chow–Künneth generation property makes pure cohomology algebraic

Let `X` be an open substack of a smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stack `X̄` over `ℂ` of pure dimension `d`, and suppose `X` has the CKgP. Prove that the cycle class map `⊕_i A^i(X) → ⊕_k W_k H^k(X)` is surjective; in particular `W_k H^k(X) = 0` for odd `k` and `W_k H^k(X)` is pure Hodge–Tate for even `k`. The same holds with `ℚ_ℓ`-coefficients over any field of characteristic zero, the closure `X̄` being smooth and proper (CLP, Lemma 4.3, pp. 11–12, and Remark 4.4, p. 12). Route: write the diagonal of `X̄ × X̄` as `Γ + Σ_ℓ Δ_ℓ` with `Γ` supported on `X̄ × (X̄ − X)` and `Δ_ℓ` sums of products `V × W` of codimensions `ℓ, d − ℓ`; for `α` lifting a class of `W_k H^k(X)`, `Δ_ℓ*α` is a cycle class and vanishes unless `k = 2d − 2ℓ`, and `Γ*α` restricts to zero on `X`.

*Needs:* §5.1; §§0.4, 0.6; MotivesAndAlgebraicCycles §0.6 (diagonal decompositions) and gap:dm-stack-cycle-class; SchemeAndStackFoundations, gap:stack-rational-chow.

**Checks.**

- Surjectivity is asserted, not injectivity: on `𝔾_m`, `A¹ = 0` and `W₂H² = 0`, while `A⁰ → W₀H⁰ = ℚ` is an isomorphism; on `ℙ¹` minus three points the statement concerns only `W₀H⁰`, not `H¹`, which has weight two.
- The proper case recovers the classical statement that the cycle class map is an isomorphism for smooth proper stacks with the CKgP (CLP, p. 11).

### 5.4 Known ranges of the Chow–Künneth generation property

For `g = 0` and every `n`, and for `g = 1, 2, 3, 4` with `n ≤ 10, 10, 11, 11` respectively, prove that `M_{g,n}` has the CKgP and `A^*(M_{g,n}) = R^*(M_{g,n})`; consequently `W_k H^k(M_{g,n}) = RH^k(M_{g,n})` and, for odd `k`, `W_k H^k(M_{g,n}) = 0` and `Gr^W_k H_c^k(M_{g,n}) = 0` (Canning–Larson, Theorem 1.4; CLP, Table 1 and Proposition 4.5, pp. 12–13). Prove also, for every `g ≥ 2` and `n ≤ 2g + 6`, that the closed substack `H_{g,n}` of pointed hyperelliptic curves has the CKgP and that every class on `M_{g,n}` supported on it is tautological, and, for every `g ≥ 4` and `n ≤ g + 7`, that the closed locus `M^{≤3}_{g,n}` of curves of gonality at most three has the same two properties (Canning–Larson, Theorem 6.1, p. 17, and Lemma 9.9, p. 33). The ranges `n ≤ 7` in genus five and `n ≤ 5` in genus six are §6.9, and the genus-seven range `n ≤ 3` is §7.7. Route, each step a part of this target, every case concluding by §5.2 (open substacks, finite stratifications, descent along proper surjective maps) and, for supported classes, by the push-pull formula. Genus zero: `M_{0,n}` is the complement of hyperplanes in `𝔸^{n−3}` (Canning–Larson, §5.1, p. 16). Genus one, `n ≤ 10`: `A^*(M_{1,n}) = R^*(M_{1,n}) = ℚ` (Belorousski, recalled as Canning–Larson, Theorem 5.1, p. 17), and the CKgP by induction on `n` through Belorousski's divisors `D_i ⊂ M_{1,n}`: each `D_i` is identified with an open substack of `M_{1,n−1}`, and a proper morphism from an open subscheme of a product of projective spaces maps onto `M_{1,n} − ⋃_i D_i` (Canning–Larson, §5.2, pp. 16–17). Hyperelliptic loci, and genus two, where `M_{2,n} = H_{2,n}`: for `n ≤ 2g + 6` the stack `H_{g,n}` has the CKgP and its Chow ring is generated by psi classes, by Canning–Larson's computation for pointed hyperelliptic curves on the binary-form presentation of EquivariantIntersectionTheory §3.3 (Canning–Larson, Theorem 6.1 and §6, p. 17), and the class of the hyperelliptic locus is tautological (Scope and ownership; Canning–Larson, proof of Corollary 7.5, p. 22). Genus three, `n ≤ 11`: the complement of `H_{3,n}` is the stack of `n`-pointed smooth plane quartics, the canonical models of non-hyperelliptic genus-three curves; for `d ≥ 4` and `n ≤ 3d − 1` the `SL₃`-lift of the stack of `n`-pointed smooth plane curves of degree `d`, a `μ₃`-gerbe over it, is an open substack of a projective bundle over an open substack of the fibre power `(ℙV)^n` over `BSL₃`, so it has the CKgP, and its Chow ring is generated by `c₂(V)`, `c₃(V)` and hyperplane classes, which restrict to polynomials in the `λ` and `ψ` classes (Canning–Larson, §7.1, p. 19, Lemmas 7.1–7.4, pp. 20–21, and Corollary 7.5, p. 22). The trigonal locus, and genus four, `n ≤ 11`: for `g ≥ 4` and `n ≤ g + 7` the Hurwitz stack `H_{3,g,n}` of pointed degree-three covers of `ℙ¹` is an open substack of a vector bundle over an open substack of the fibre power `(ℙE^∨)^n` over the stack of rank-two bundles `E` of degree `g + 2` on `ℙ¹`-bundles, so it has the CKgP, and its Chow ring is generated over the subring generated by `κ₁` and the psi classes by the monomials in the classes `ζ_i` of the loci where the `i`-th marking is a ramification point (Canning–Larson, §9.2 and Lemmas 9.1–9.7, pp. 29–32), whose pushforwards are classes of loci with marked ramification and so are tautological (Scope and ownership; Canning–Larson, Lemma 9.8, p. 33, through Lemma 8.1, p. 26); with the hyperelliptic step this gives the trigonal clause (Lemma 9.9), and every genus-four curve has gonality at most three (Lemma 9.11, p. 33). Finally §5.3 gives the cohomological consequence, and by the complementary-degree duality of the Conventions, `Gr^W_k H_c^k(M_{g,n})` is dual to `W_{2d−k} H^{2d−k}(M_{g,n})(d)`, which vanishes because `2d − k` is odd as well.

*Needs:* §§5.2, 5.3; §§0.1, 0.6; EquivariantIntersectionTheory §§2.1–2.3 (equivariant Chow groups of the quotient presentations) and §3.3 (the binary-form presentation of the hyperelliptic stack); CanonicalModelsAndGonality §§3.4 and 9.1 (the canonical model of a non-hyperelliptic curve of genus three); WeilConjectures, gap:open-dm-stack-duality; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (the complementary-degree duality in both realizations).

**Checks.**

- The bounds are not uniform in the genus: `c(2) = 10` while `c(3) = c(4) = 11`; and `c(1) = 10` because `H¹¹(M̄_{1,11}) ≠ 0` forces `W₁₁H¹¹(M_{1,11}) ≠ 0`.
- At `(1, 11)` the conclusion fails, consistent with the bound: `W₁₁H¹¹(M_{1,11}) = S₁₂` is not algebraic.
- The compact-support clause uses the complementary degree: at `(1, 10)`, `d = 10`, `Gr^W_{11} H_c^{11}` is dual to `W₉H⁹(M_{1,10})(10) = 0`; at `(1, 11)`, `d = 11` and `2d − 11 = 11`, so `Gr^W_{11} H_c^{11}(M_{1,11}) ≅ (W₁₁H¹¹(M_{1,11}))^∨(−11) ≅ S₁₂`, of rank two, the pair lying outside the CKgP range.
- Each bound is the one its construction allows: `2g + 6 = 10` for pointed hyperelliptic curves of genus two, `3d − 1 = 11` for pointed plane quartics, and `g + 7 = 11` for pointed trigonal covers in genus four.
- The trigonal clause at `g = 7` allows `n ≤ 14`, the range of §6.2, and the hyperelliptic clause there allows `n ≤ 20`; at `g = 4` the trigonal clause covers all of `M_{4,n}`, every genus-four curve having gonality at most three.

### Examples

`M_{0,n}` has the CKgP for every `n`, being an open subset of affine space minus hyperplanes. The diagonal of `ℙ¹` is `[pt × ℙ¹] + [ℙ¹ × pt]`, a sum of exterior products; that of an elliptic curve is not.

### Dependencies

Layer 0; SchemeAndStackFoundations, gap:stack-rational-chow; EquivariantIntersectionTheory §§2.1–2.3 and 3.3; CanonicalModelsAndGonality §§3.4 and 9.1; MotivesAndAlgebraicCycles §0.6 with gap:dm-stack-cycle-class; WeilConjectures, gap:open-dm-stack-duality.

## Layer 6: The tetragonal and low-gonality loci

The complement of the pentagonal locus in `M_{7,n}` is covered by the low-gonality and tetragonal strata. A class being algebraic is not enough: its pushforward to `M_{7,n}` must be tautological. The tetragonal Hurwitz stack (§6.3) and its tautological pushforwards (§6.5) are stated for every genus; in genera five and six they give the Chow–Künneth ranges of §6.9.

### 6.1 Gonality strata in genus seven

Inside `M_{7,n}` let `M^{≤a}_{7,n}` be the locus of curves admitting a finite morphism of degree at most `a` to `ℙ¹` over the algebraic closure, and `M^{a}_{7,n} = M^{≤a}_{7,n} − M^{≤a−1}_{7,n}`. Prove that the loci `M^{≤a}` are closed and nested, that every genus-seven curve has gonality at most five, and that `M_{7,n} = M^{≤3}_{7,n} ⊔ M^{4}_{7,n} ⊔ M^{5}_{7,n}`; write `M°_{7,n} = M^5_{7,n}` for the open pentagonal locus (CLP, §5, opening, pp. 13–14). Route: relative linear series and properness of the relevant Hurwitz morphisms give closedness; the bound at a geometric point is the case `g = 7` of the Brill–Noether gonality bound `gon ≤ ⌊(g + 3)/2⌋ = 5` for the function field of the curve over an algebraically closed field (CanonicalModelsAndGonality §5.4, from `ρ(7, 1, 5) = 1 ≥ 0`), carried to the curve by the dictionary of AlgebraicCurves Layer 12, under which a nonconstant function `f` with `[F : K(f)] = a` is a finite morphism of degree `a` to `ℙ¹`.

*Needs:* StableReductionPartII §§0.4 and 7.11; CanonicalModelsAndGonality §5.4 (Brill–Noether existence and the gonality bound); AlgebraicCurves Layer 12 (function fields and smooth proper curves, maps to `ℙ¹`); SchemeAndStackFoundations §3.2 (Riemann–Hurwitz and degree bounds on curves).

API:

- The loci `M^{≤a}_{7,n}` are closed and nested for `a = 2, 3, 4, 5`, with `M^{≤5}_{7,n} = M_{7,n}`.
- Gonality strata with markings are the inverse images of the unmarked strata under the forgetful map.
- `M^{≤3}`, `M^4` and `M^5` partition `M_{7,n}`.
- A geometric pointed curve lies in `M^a_{7,n}` if and only if it admits a degree-`a` map to `ℙ¹` and no map of smaller positive degree.

**Checks.**

- A hyperelliptic genus-seven curve lies in `M^{≤3}` and not in `M^4`.
- A curve of gonality exactly four lies outside the pentagonal open `M°_{7,n}`.
- Adding a marking does not change gonality: the strata of `M_{7,n}` are pulled back from `M_7`.
- The bound five is the first degree the Brill–Noether number allows: `ρ(7, 1, 5) = 7 − 2·3 = 1 ≥ 0`, while `ρ(7, 1, 4) = 7 − 2·4 = −1 < 0` gives no degree-four map on every curve; a bound `⌊(g + 2)/2⌋ = 4` in its place would make the pentagonal stratum `M°_{7,n}` of §§7.3–7.7 empty. Hyperelliptic curves show that the bound is not an equality.

### 6.2 The low-gonality locus

For `n ≤ 14` prove that `M^{≤3}_{7,n}` has the CKgP and that every Chow class on `M_{7,n}` supported on `M^{≤3}_{7,n}` is tautological (Canning–Larson, Lemma 9.9; CLP, §5.1, p. 13). The intrinsic Chow ring of the stratum and the classes it supports on `M_{7,n}` are distinguished. Route: the trigonal clause of §5.4 at `g = 7`, valid for `n ≤ g + 7 = 14`, which contains the hyperelliptic clause for `n ≤ 2g + 6 = 20`; the locus `M^{≤3}_{7,n}` of §6.1 is the locus of that clause.

*Needs:* §6.1; §§5.1–5.2, 5.4; §§0.1, 0.6.

**Checks.**

- The support statement is the one that extends tautological generation from the complement: a class of `A^*(M^{≤3}_{7,n})` must push forward to a tautological class, which algebraicity alone does not give.
- The hyperelliptic locus is included in `M^{≤3}_{7,n}`.

### 6.3 The tetragonal Hurwitz stack and its Casnati–Ekedahl bundles

For `g ≥ 2` let `H_{4,g,n}` be the stack of degree-four covers `C → ℙ¹` by smooth curves of genus `g` with `n` distinct ordered marked points, modulo automorphisms of the target, and `H^◇_{4,g,n}` the open substack where `C` has gonality exactly four. The Casnati–Ekedahl structure theorem attaches to a cover a rank-three bundle `E` and a rank-two bundle `F` on `ℙ¹`, each of degree `g + 3`, with a canonical isomorphism `det E ≅ det F`, such that `C ⊂ ℙE^∨` is the zero locus of a section of `F^∨ ⊗ O(2)`. Write `B` for the stack of such pairs `(E, F)` of bundles of ranks three and two and degree `g + 3` on `ℙ¹`-fibrations, with an isomorphism `det E ≅ det F`, `ℙ → B` for its universal `ℙ¹`-fibration carrying the universal bundles, and `H_{4,g} → B` for the morphism recording the Casnati–Ekedahl pair of a cover; the universal curve `C` over `H_{4,g}` is a finite flat degree-four cover `α : C → ℙ` of the pulled-back fibration (CLP, §5.2, p. 14). Construct these data over the `SL₂`-lift of the target on which `O(1)`, `E` and `F` exist, and the forgetful morphism `β′_n : H^◇_{4,g,n} → M_{g,n} − M^{≤3}_{g,n}`, proper with image `M^4_{g,n}` (CLP, §5.2, pp. 13–14; Canning–Larson genus seven, §§3–4).

Construct also the framed presentation of the `SL₂`-lift. For a degree-four cover `α : C → ℙ¹` by a smooth connected curve of genus `g`, over a field of characteristic zero, the trace splits `α_*O_C = O ⊕ E^∨` and `h⁰(E^∨) = h⁰(O_C) − 1 = 0`, so every summand of `E` has degree at least one, `E` is globally generated, `h¹(E) = 0` and `h⁰(E) = deg E + rank E = g + 6`. Let `Fr_{g,n}` parametrize such covers of the fixed line `ℙ¹` with `n` distinct ordered marked points and a basis of `H⁰(ℙ¹, E)`. Prove that `Fr_{g,n}` is a smooth quasi-projective scheme with an ample invertible sheaf linearised for `G = SL₂ × GL_{g+6}`, that `[Fr_{g,n}/G]` is the `SL₂`-lift of `H_{4,g,n}`, and that `ℙ¹ × Fr_{g,n}` and the universal curve over `Fr_{g,n}` present its `ℙ¹`-fibration and universal curve. An automorphism of a cover over `ℙ¹` that fixes a basis of `H⁰(E)` acts trivially on `E`, which the basis generates, hence on `α_*O_C`, and so is the identity: framed covers have no automorphisms, while unframed covers of the fixed line can have them, and the parametrized Hurwitz space of Canning–Larson genus seven, Remark 2.3, pp. 6–7, is not a scheme (Corrections to the sources). Route: `Fr_{g,n}` is a `GL_{g+6}`-torsor, the frame bundle of `π_*E`, over the stack of pointed covers of the fixed line, which is smooth because the normal sheaf of a finite cover of a curve is torsion; the basis embeds `ℙE^∨` in `ℙ¹ × ℙ^{g+5}`, and with it the Casnati–Ekedahl embedding of `C`, so that `Fr_{g,n}` is the `𝔾_m`-torsor of scalings of the basis over a locally closed subscheme of a fibre power of the universal family over a Hilbert scheme of `ℙ¹ × ℙ^{g+5}` (SchemeAndStackFoundations §4.14), on which `G` acts through its linear action on `ℙ¹ × ℙ^{g+5}`.

*Needs:* §6.1; SchemeAndStackFoundations §§1.18–1.23 (stacks and quotients) and §4.14 (Hilbert schemes); AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry §1.3 (Grassmannians and projective bundles of quotients); StableReductionPartII §§2.248 and 7.11.

API:

- Forgetting the cover is proper from `H^◇_{4,7,n}` onto `M^4_{7,n}`.
- `E`, `F` and the isomorphism `det E ≅ det F` pull back along base change of families.
- The universal curve embeds in `ℙE^∨` as the zero locus of the universal section of `F^∨ ⊗ O(2)`.
- Base change of finite flat covers and marked sections induces pullback of Hurwitz families and of their Casnati–Ekedahl data on the `SL₂`-lift.
- An isomorphism of cover data is a commuting square of source and target isomorphisms preserving ordered sections; automorphisms of the target are retained.
- The framed covers `Fr_{g,n}` form a smooth quasi-projective scheme with a `G`-linearised ample invertible sheaf, `G = SL₂ × GL_{g+6}`; `[Fr_{g,n}/G]` is the `SL₂`-lift of `H_{4,g,n}`, with `ℙ¹ × Fr_{g,n}` and the universal curve over `Fr_{g,n}` presenting its fibration and universal curve, and the restriction to the inverse image of `H^◇_{4,g,n}` is open.

**Checks.**

- At `g = 7` the ranks are `3` and `2` and both degrees are `10`; `h⁰(E) = 10 + 3 = 13 = g + 6`.
- Unframed covers of a fixed line can have automorphisms. A bielliptic curve `C → E′ → ℙ¹` of genus seven, through a double cover `γ` of an elliptic curve `E′` and a double cover `β : E′ → ℙ¹`, has gonality four: by Castelnuovo–Severi a second map of degree two would force `g ≤ 2·1 + 0 + 1 = 3`, and one of degree three `g ≤ 2 + 0 + 2 = 4`. The involution `ι` of `C` over `E′` is an automorphism of the cover over `ℙ¹`. With `γ_*O_C = O ⊕ L^{−1}`, `E^∨ = O(−2) ⊕ β_*L^{−1}` and `ι` acts by `−1` on the rank-two summand `β_*L^{−1}`, whose dual has nonzero sections; so `ι` moves every basis of `H⁰(E)`, and the framed cover has no automorphism while the unframed cover of the fixed line has `ι`.
- A degree-four map factoring through a hyperelliptic double cover is excluded from `H^◇` by the gonality condition.
- Exchanging the two defining quadrics changes a frame of `F` and not the cover.

### 6.4 Splitting strata of the tetragonal Hurwitz stack

In `H_{4,7,0}` let `X` be the locus with `E = O(3) ⊕ O(3) ⊕ O(4)` and `F` of type `(5, 5)` or `(4, 6)`; `Σ₂` the locus with `E = (2, 4, 4)`, `F = (4, 6)`; `Σ₃` the locus with `E = (2, 3, 5)`, `F = (4, 6)`; and `Z` the maximally unbalanced locus, which parametrizes hyperelliptic curves. Subscript `n` denotes the inverse image in `H_{4,7,n}`. Prove that `H^◇_{4,7,n} = X_n ⊔ Σ_{2,n} ⊔ Σ_{3,n}`, with `Y_n = Σ_{2,n} ⊔ Σ_{3,n}` closed (CLP, §5.2, pp. 14–15; Canning–Larson genus seven, §4.4). Route: semicontinuity of splitting types and the constraints from smoothness and irreducibility of `C`; each stratum is a quotient of an open set of equations by `SL₂ ⋉ (Aut O(e⃗) × Aut O(f⃗))`.

*Needs:* §6.3.

API:

- `X`, `Σ₂`, `Σ₃` form a finite stratification of `H^◇_{4,7,0}`; `Y = Σ₂ ⊔ Σ₃` is closed in it.
- The marked strata are the inverse images of the unmarked strata.
- Each listed splitting type has the rank and degree of `E` and `F`.
- Within `H^◇`, a geometric cover lies in `X`, `Σ₂` or `Σ₃` exactly when its pair of splitting types is the displayed one.

**Checks.**

- `3 + 3 + 4 = 10`, `5 + 5 = 4 + 6 = 10`, `2 + 4 + 4 = 2 + 3 + 5 = 10`.
- `Σ₂` and `Σ₃` are distinct although their `F` splitting types agree.
- The hyperelliptic stratum `Z` is not part of `H^◇`; its image lies in `M^{≤3}_{7,n}`.

### 6.5 Casnati–Ekedahl pushforwards are tautological

For `g ≥ 2`, suppose `x ∈ A^*(H^◇_{4,g,n})` lies in the image of `A^*((ℙE^∨)^n) → A^*(H^◇_{4,g,n})` along the map recording the images of the markings. Prove that `β′_{n*}x` is tautological on `M_{g,n} − M^{≤3}_{g,n}` (CLP, Lemma 5.1, p. 14, quoting Canning–Larson, Lemma 10.5). Route: expand in the hyperplane classes `z_i`, `ζ_i` and the Chern classes of `E`, `F`; push forward along the cover incidence and use Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch for the cover `α : C → ℙ` and the fibration `ℙ → H_{4,g,n}` to express the results in kappa, psi and lambda classes.

*Needs:* §§6.3, 0.1, 0.2, 0.6; SchemeAndStackFoundations, gap:stack-rational-chow (proper pushforward); EquivariantIntersectionTheory, gap:equivariant-grr (Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch for the cover `α : C → ℙ` and the fibration `ℙ → H_{4,g,n}`, induced by `G`-equivariant projective morphisms of the framed presentation of §6.3).

**Checks.**

- The conclusion is tautological, not merely algebraic: a statement that every pushed-forward class is algebraic would not feed §7.7.
- For `n = 0` and `x = 1` the pushforward is the fundamental class of the tetragonal locus, which is tautological.

### 6.6 The open tetragonal stratum with at most three markings

For `0 ≤ n ≤ 3` prove that `X_n` has the CKgP and that `A^*((ℙE^∨)^n) → A^*(X_n)` is surjective (CLP, Lemma 5.2, p. 14, through Canning–Larson, Lemmas 10.11 and 10.12). Route: present the equations vanishing at `n` points as a vector-bundle kernel over the `n`-fold incidence; evaluation at `n ≤ 3` distinct points of a smooth curve is surjective onto a rank-`2n` target; restrict to the smooth open and apply §5.2.

*Needs:* §§6.4, 5.2.

**Checks.**

- The evaluation target has rank `2n`, not `n`: `W = γ^*F^∨ ⊗ O(2)` has rank two.
- No conclusion is drawn for `n = 4`, where evaluation can fail to be surjective.

### 6.7 Chow generation on the special tetragonal strata

For `ℓ = 2, 3` and `n ≤ 3` prove that `A^*(Σ_{ℓ,n})` is generated over `A^*(Σ_ℓ)` by the classes `z_i, ζ_i`, that `Σ_{ℓ,n}` has the CKgP, and that `A^*((ℙE^∨)^n) → A^*(Σ_{ℓ,n})` is surjective (CLP, Lemmas 5.3 and 5.5, pp. 15–16; Canning–Larson genus seven, Lemmas 3.10, 4.2 and 4.3). Route: `Σ_ℓ` is an open substack of a vector bundle over the locally closed substack of `B` (§6.3) of pairs of bundles of fixed splitting type; with markings it is open in the restriction of the kernel of the evaluation map to the locus where evaluation is surjective; the base generators are restrictions of Chern classes of `E`, `F`.

*Needs:* §§6.4, 5.2.

**Checks.**

- `Σ₃` (with `E = (2,3,5)`) lies in the closure of `Σ₂` (with `E = (2,4,4)`), being more unbalanced; so `Σ_{3,n}` is closed in `Y_n` and the localization of §6.8 removes it first. Treating `Y_n` as a single stratum with one splitting type would be wrong.
- `A^*(B) → A^*(Σ_ℓ)` is surjective for both values of `ℓ`, for the stack `B` of bundle pairs of §6.3, from different lemmas (4.2 and 4.3(1) of Canning–Larson genus seven).

### 6.8 The tetragonal locus

For `n ≤ 3` prove that `H^◇_{4,7,n}` and `M^4_{7,n}` have the CKgP, that `A^*((ℙE^∨)^n) → A^*(H^◇_{4,7,n})` is surjective, and that every class on `M_{7,n}` supported on `M^4_{7,n}` is tautological modulo classes supported on `M^{≤3}_{7,n}` (CLP, Corollary 5.4 and Lemmas 5.6–5.7, pp. 16–17). Route: localization along `Y_n ⊂ H^◇` and `Σ_{3,n} ⊂ Y_n`; the fundamental classes `[Y_n]`, `[Σ_{3,n}]` are pulled back from `B` (Canning–Larson genus seven, Lemma 4.8), so the projection formula lifts the supported terms; descend the CKgP along the proper map `β′_n` and push classes forward with §6.5.

*Needs:* §§6.5–6.7; §5.2; §6.2.

**Checks.**

- Surjectivity on each stratum is not enough: the fundamental classes of the closed strata must themselves be restrictions from `(ℙE^∨)^n`.
- Proper surjective maps are surjective on rational Chow groups, which is how classes supported on `M^4_{7,n}` come from `H^◇_{4,7,n}`.

### 6.9 Chow–Künneth generation in genera five and six

For `g = 5` and `n ≤ 7`, and for `g = 6` and `n ≤ 5`, prove that `M_{g,n}` has the CKgP and `A^*(M_{g,n}) = R^*(M_{g,n})`; consequently `W_k H^k(M_{g,n}) = RH^k(M_{g,n})` and, for odd `k`, `W_k H^k(M_{g,n}) = 0` and `Gr^W_k H_c^k(M_{g,n}) = 0` (Canning–Larson, Theorem 1.4, and Lemmas 10.13–10.14, p. 39; CLP, Table 1 and Proposition 4.5, pp. 12–13). Route: every case concludes by §5.2 (open substacks, finite stratifications, descent along proper surjective maps) and, for supported classes, by the push-pull formula. In the tetragonal Hurwitz stack of §6.3, the union of the Casnati–Ekedahl strata on which the smaller summand of `F` has degree at least four is, for `n ≤ 17 − 2g`, an open substack of a vector bundle over an open substack of `(ℙE^∨)^n`, so it has the CKgP and its Chow ring is the image of `A^*((ℙE^∨)^n)` (Canning–Larson, Lemmas 10.11–10.12, p. 39); it is the complement of the trigonal locus in genus five and of the trigonal and plane-quintic loci in genus six (Lemmas 10.9–10.10, p. 38); and §6.5 makes the pushforwards of its classes tautological. The trigonal locus is the trigonal clause of §5.4, valid for `n ≤ g + 7`. The plane-quintic locus, the image of the stack of pointed smooth plane quintics, is the plane-curve step of §5.4 with `d = 5` and `n ≤ 14`, its class being tautological (Scope and ownership; Lemmas 10.13–10.14, p. 39). Finally §5.3 and the complementary-degree duality of the Conventions give the cohomological consequence, as in §5.4.

*Needs:* §§5.2–5.4 (permanence, the cohomological consequence, and the trigonal clause and plane-curve step); §§6.3 and 6.5 (the tetragonal Hurwitz stack and its tautological pushforwards); §§0.1, 0.6; WeilConjectures, gap:open-dm-stack-duality; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory, gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge (the complementary-degree duality in both realizations).

**Checks.**

- The tetragonal strata, not the trigonal clause, set the bounds: with `f = 4` the bound `4f − 2g + 1` of Canning–Larson, Lemma 10.11, is `17 − 2g`, which is `7` at `g = 5` and `5` at `g = 6`, while the trigonal clause alone would allow `g + 7 = 12` and `13` markings.
- The method gives at most seven markings in every genus: the smaller summand of `F` has degree `f₁ ≤ (g + 3)/2`, so `4f₁ − 2g + 1 ≤ 7` (Canning–Larson, Remark 10.7, p. 38); at `(5, 8)` no conclusion is drawn.
- The plane-quintic locus occurs in genus six only, a smooth plane quintic having genus `(5 − 1)(5 − 2)/2 = 6`, and its step allows `3·5 − 1 = 14 ≥ 5` markings, so it does not limit `c(6)`; in genus five the image of those strata is the complement of the trigonal locus alone.

### Examples

For `n = 0`, `X_0` is an open subset of a vector bundle over the substack of `B` of bundle pairs of types `(3,3,4)` and `(5,5)` or `(4,6)`; its Chow ring is generated by Chern classes of `E` and `F`.

### Dependencies

Layers 0 and 5; StableReductionPartII §§0.4, 2.248, 7.11; SchemeAndStackFoundations §§1.18–1.23, §§3.2 and 4.14, with gap:stack-rational-chow; EquivariantIntersectionTheory with gap:equivariant-grr; CanonicalModelsAndGonality §5.4; AlgebraicCurves Layer 12; AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry §1.3; WeilConjectures, gap:open-dm-stack-duality; MixedHodgeModulesAndIrregularHodgeTheory with gap:dm-stack-mixed-hodge.

## Layer 7: The orthogonal Grassmannian and the Mukai model of pentagonal genus-seven curves

The half-spin representation is available algebraically (`TauCeti.spinPlus`). The work here is the family-level comparison with curve moduli and the descent of bundles and Chow classes through central quotients. The half-spin representation `S⁺` of `Spin₁₀` is not a representation of `SO₁₀`: the central element `−1` of `Spin₁₀` acts on `S⁺` by `−1`. Only the projective action descends, to `PSO₁₀ = Spin₁₀/μ₄`. The printed model (CLP, §5.3, equations (5.10)–(5.13), pp. 20–21) treats `S⁺` as a vector bundle on `BSO₁₀`; the targets below replace it by the `Spin₁₀`-lift and a rigidification, with the transfer of rational Chow groups proved separately.

### 7.1 Chow ring of the universal maximal isotropic Grassmannian

For a split nondegenerate quadratic space of dimension ten over a field of characteristic zero, fix one connected component `OG⁺(5, 10)` of its maximal isotropic Grassmannian. Prove that `[OG⁺(5, 10)/SO₁₀] ≅ BP` for the stabilizer `P` of a maximal isotropic subspace, a parabolic subgroup with Levi factor `GL₅` and unipotent radical isomorphic to `𝔾_a^{10}` (skew-symmetric `5 × 5` matrices); that `BGL₅ → BP` is an affine bundle; and hence that `BP` has the CKgP with `A^*(BP) = ℚ[c₁(U), …, c₅(U)]` for the tautological rank-five subbundle `U` (CLP, Lemma 5.8 and Remark 5.9, pp. 18–19). On `BP` the quadratic bundle `V` sits in the exact sequence `0 → U → V → U^∨ → 0`; the printed description `V = U ⊕ U^∨` holds after pullback to `BGL₅` but not `P`-equivariantly, and only the exact sequence is used.

*Needs:* §§5.2, 0.6; `TauCeti.spinPlus` and `TauCeti.finrank_spinPlus` (for the identification of the component with the spinor variety); SchemeAndStackFoundations, gap:stack-rational-chow; EquivariantIntersectionTheory §§2.2–2.3 (homotopy invariance for the affine bundle `BGL₅ → BP` and `A^*_{GL₅} = ℤ[c₁, …, c₅]`).

**Checks.**

- The unipotent radical has dimension `10 = 5·4/2`, so `BGL₅ → BP` is an affine bundle of rank ten, and `dim OG⁺(5, 10) = dim SO₁₀ − dim P = 45 − (25 + 10) = 10`.
- The union of both components of the maximal isotropic Grassmannian is disconnected and is not the image of the spinor embedding, which sees one component.

### 7.2 Fibre powers of the orthogonal Grassmann bundle

For `n ≥ 1` let `OG(5, V)^n` be the `n`-fold fibre power over `BSO₁₀` of the fixed component of the orthogonal Grassmann bundle of the universal oriented quadratic bundle `V`. Prove that `OG(5, V)^n` has the CKgP and that its rational Chow ring is generated by the Chern classes of the tautological subbundles `U₁, …, U_n` (CLP, Lemma 5.10 and Corollary 5.11, p. 19). Route: for `n > 1`, `OG(5, V)^n` is the orthogonal Grassmann bundle of the pulled-back quadratic bundle over `OG(5, V)^{n−1}`, which carries the tautological isotropic subbundle `U_{n−1}`; reduce the structure group to the parabolic `P` and argue with the type-D Giambelli formula on the split fibre and a splitting-principle descent through the parabolic reduction. The printed proof of CLP Lemma 5.10 trivializes the underlying vector bundle on strata and treats the quadratic form as split there; a trivialization of the vector bundle does not trivialize a quadratic form, and the argument must use the parabolic reduction instead.

*Needs:* §§7.1, 5.2; SchemeAndStackFoundations, gap:stack-rational-chow.

**Checks.**

- `n = 0` is excluded: the empty fibre product is `BSO₁₀`, whose rational Chow ring is not generated by an empty list of Chern classes (it contains the Pontryagin and Euler classes).
- A nondegenerate quadratic form over a field need not be split even when its vector space is trivial: `x² + y²` over `ℝ` is anisotropic. The stratification argument must therefore keep track of the form, not only the bundle.
- For `n = 1` the statement is §7.1.

### 7.3 The Mukai model and its comparison with curve moduli

For `0 ≤ n ≤ 4` let `Z_n ⊂ OG(5, V)^n` be the open locus where the tautological lines `L₁, …, L_n ⊂ S⁺` are independent, constructed on the `Spin₁₀`-lift where `S⁺` is a vector bundle, and let `I_n` be the locally closed locus of `(p₁, …, p_n, Λ)` with `Λ` a seven-dimensional subspace of `S⁺` containing the lines `L_i` and with `ℙΛ ∩ OG⁺(5, 10)` a smooth curve. Prove that the rigidification of `[I_n/Spin₁₀]` by the central `μ₄`, which acts trivially on `I_n`, is equivalent to `M°_{7,n}`, compatibly with forgetting markings and preserving automorphism groups of pointed curves (CLP, §5.3, equations (5.10)–(5.13) and Lemma 5.13, pp. 20–21; Mukai, the genus-seven linear-section theorem, recalled in CLP §5.3.2). Route: Mukai's canonical construction `W = ker(Sym² H⁰(ω) → H⁰(ω²))`, a ten-dimensional space with a canonical nondegenerate quadratic form on `W^∨`, and the five-dimensional isotropic subspaces `W_p^⊥` from `ω(−p)`, give the map from pointed curves; the inverse sends `(p_i, Λ)` to `ℙΛ ∩ OG⁺(5, 10)`; independence of the marked points for `n ≤ 4` follows because a pentagonal curve has no `g¹_n` with `n ≤ 4`. The rigidification is AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry §5.1.

*Needs:* §6.1; §7.2; `TauCeti.spinPlus`, `TauCeti.spinPlusAction`, `TauCeti.spinPlusSubrep`, `TauCeti.finrank_spinPlus`; AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry §1.3 (Grassmannians of quotients) and §5.1 (rigidification); StableReductionPartII §0.4.

**Checks.**
